(A5) The compiled prelude (`src/llvm/prelude.strata`, `strata.compiled`, read by `main` from the current folder, typed by `check.typed`) is compiled with the program, its functions without call-stack frames as `llvm.rs`'s; comprehensions and `Set { }` / `Dict { }` literals become its calls as `src/desugar.rs` does. (A6) Core's built-ins, each as `llvm.rs` compiles it: runtime.c's string, number and parsing functions, the prelude's `str_…`, `Option` results built in place; `to_upper` / `to_lower` / `is_alpha` from the same Unicode tables (`compiler/unicode`, data generated from Rust's standard library by `tools/codegen_unicode.rs`, as `llvm.rs` makes them). (A7) Sets and dicts as sorted lists with the prelude's `set_…` / `dict_…`, given a structural order per type (`@"cmp:T"`: numbers, floats as `total_cmp`, strings by bytes, tuples and lists element by element, a record or variant by variant in the order declared then field by field: tags are now numbered in declaration order, so comparing two tags compares variants), also used by `sort` and `sort_by`. (`<` stays for numbers only: the checker refuses it on anything else, so the order is never `<`'s.) (A8) Format specs through `strata_format`, with `llvm.rs`'s faults. Tested as before (`tests/codegen.rs`: closures and generics, a generic result from the type wanted, built-ins with sets, dicts and formats, Unicode), each program's executable as `strata build`'s and freeing everything. Not yet, waiting on other items: `matches` (its regex is compiled while building: compile-time code, C19), channels (concurrency, B16). Found on the way: a private function in one module captures calls to a built-in of the same name in other modules (`other.strata` with a private `fn slice(a: Int)`: `main`'s `slice([1, 2, 3], 0, 1)` is refused as a call to `other.slice`), a resolver bug, not fixed here. 

  **Questions for the user (codegen plan)** (decisions taken while working through the plan without asking, as asked on 2026-11-23; each is the default easiest to change later):
  - Capabilities (`Terminal`, `Disk`, the program's own) are compiled as records with no fields, one small allocation each, where `llvm.rs` passes a null pointer; the behaviour is the same. Keep, or match `llvm.rs`'s null?
  - A channel nothing is ever sent on (only closed) gets elements of type `()` when the checker can't say otherwise. Fine?
  - A variable declared with a type not known whole (`mut xs := []`, when the checker gives no type) takes the type its first assignment gives it, instead of codegen refusing the program. Fine?
  - Codegen doesn't enforce check-level rules the Rust build enforces at build time (e.g. `main` asking for `RunMode` without `[run] mode = true` compiles). Should `compiler codegen` refuse such programs, or is that the checker's job only?
  - New flags: `strata test --strata` / `--compare` (tests through compiler/codegen and the JIT harness) and `verify --jit --strata` (fuzz drivers from compiler/codegen, scripts only). Names and scope OK?
  - The Strata checker (`compiler/check`) was taught more so codegen gets types: `select` / `race` arms, `spawn supervised`, a tuple `let`'s type, `Sender` / `Receiver`, `for` over iterators. Its errors are unchanged (tests/check.rs passes). OK to keep these in the checker?
  - Two bugs fixed outside codegen on the way: `strata_host_run` leaked the OS's message (runtime.c), `deps.module_files` took a package's `.strata` cache folder for a module.
  - A union with refined members (`type Id = Small | String`): **decided (user, 2026-11-23): checked on a `let` and an assignment too**, not matching `llvm.rs` (being abandoned): `let j: Id = 50` faults `let binding: 50 is not a `Small | String``. Built (branch `codegen-c`): a value not yet made a union is checked against the members of its own type. Found on the way: an assignment to a union variable stored the member's value unconverted (`k = 7` with `mut k: Id`: invalid IR); it is made a union now, as a `let`'s value is.
  - C22's switch-over: **decided (user, 2026-11-23): `build` and `test` default to compiler/codegen**, `verify` stays on Rust. Built (branch `codegen-c`): `strata build PATH` and `strata --file F test` go through compiler/codegen (`compare::command`), and where it doesn't cover the program (or clang refuses its IR) say so in a `note:` and use `llvm.rs`; `--rust` or `STRATA_BACKEND=rust` asks for `llvm.rs`; `--emit-llvm`, `test`'s `--with-deps` / `--jit` / `--interpret`, a codebase (no path) and the compiler package itself stay Rust's. The recursion trap: the compiler package's own build (`compare::built`, and any `build` of `compiler/`) is always Rust's (`--rust` and `STRATA_BACKEND=rust` on the inner process), never compiler/codegen's, which it makes. Cargo's tests are pinned to Rust (`.cargo/config.toml`'s `[env]`), so tests/build.rs and tests/cli.rs keep testing `llvm.rs` until it is deleted; `build_and_test_use_compiler_codegen_by_default` (tests/codegen.rs) checks the default and the fallback. Other agents told through CLAUDE.md.
  - `[native]` paths: **decided (user, 2026-11-23)**: `[native]` takes `include_dirs` and `lib_dirs` (relative to the package or absolute; a library folder is also the run-time search path). Built (branch `codegen-c`): `compiler/strata.toml` links `libs = ["LLVM-C"]` from Homebrew's folders, and `compiler/c/jit.c` includes `<llvm-c/...>` instead of declaring LLVM's functions, and sets up the machine's own target (`LLVMInitializeNativeTarget`), no longer AArch64 only. Still Homebrew's path: another machine edits it (or, later, `llvm-config` fills it in).
  - `--trace` through compiler/codegen: **built (branch `codegen-c`)** without a table of value shapes for Rust: `compiler codegen --trace` / `compiler jit --trace` (what `strata run --strata --jit --trace` runs) shows each framed call's arguments and its result with the compiled code's own `debug` helpers and hands the strings to runtime.c's `strata_trace_enter` / `strata_trace_leave`, which indent by depth; the output is `strata run --trace`'s, character for character (tests/codegen.rs, jit_traces_as_rust_traces). Plain `strata run --trace` stays Rust's until `run` goes through the JIT by default.
  - **C21, precompiled std: built (branch `codegen-c`, user 2026-11-23).** `compiler codegen --library` compiles std's functions that are the same in every program (not generic, no compile-time parameter, no trait value in or out, not foreign; those reaching something not compiled yet are left out, by the calls recorded while compiling, and it compiles again without them) into IR whose only exported symbols are those functions (`; precompiled NAME` lines first; its helpers, instances and lambdas `internal`), compiled as if tasks were running (it runs in any program). `codegen --precompiled NAMES` declares them instead of compiling them. For this, tags are stable (`stable_tag`: a hash of the type's name times 4096 plus the constructor's place; a union box's 2^40 plus a hash of the member type), as `llvm.rs`'s, instead of numbered per program. `strata` keeps it beside the built compiler (`~/.cache/strata/compiler/<hash>/std/`: `std.o`, `libstd.dylib`, `names`; `STRATA_NO_STD_LIBRARY` turns it off): `build` links `std.o` statically, `run --strata --jit` passes `compiler jit --load libstd.dylib --precompiled names` (c/jit.c's `jit_load`, `dlopen` global, so LLJIT's process lookup finds it). 311 functions. Measured on examples/page: a default `build` 7.6 s → 1.0 s, a JIT run 2 min 49 s → 0.9 s (LLJIT on the whole std's IR was the cost); the same output. `strata test`'s path (Rust's JIT harness) doesn't use it yet. Tested: tests/codegen.rs, std_precompiled_once_links_with_programs (wc and page, linked with the library, as compiled whole).
  - **Assigning a list's element, `xs[i] = v`: decided with the user 2026-11-25, built (branch `index-assign`).** Values never change, so on a `mut` variable, a `mut` parameter or a field of one, `xs[i] = v` means `xs = set_at(xs, i, v)` (a core built-in, `requires 0 <= i && i < len(xs)`, also callable as a function): the tree keeps `Assign { place: Index { .. } }` as written (no new node, no format change; the parser accepts an element in a place, the printer gives it back, `+=` included), and `desugar` rewrites it before the checker, `verify`, codegen and eval see it, nested places nesting (`s.xs[i] = v` is `s.xs = set_at(s.xs, i, v)`; `grid[r][c] = v` is `grid = set_at(grid, r, set_at(grid[r], c, v))`; `xs[i].f = v` is `xs = set_at(xs, i, { mut $elem_0 := xs[i]; $elem_0.f = v; $elem_0 })`), an index other than a variable or a literal named first (`$index_0`) so it runs once. The checker reports errors as written (`assigning `xs[0]`: expected `Int`, found `String``; `s[0] = …` on a `String`). `verify`: the index is an obligation ("index in range", proven or a runtime fault, left out of the build when proven, as for `xs[i]`), and the new list is `take(xs, i) + [v] + drop(xs, i + 1)` in the prover's list model (GConcat/GShift/GValues), so it is known by position: length unchanged (loops over `len(xs)` stay proven), element `i` is `v`, the others the old ones, and a declared `List[Positive]` needs only `v` proven. Codegen: `x = set_at(x, …)` (a variable, or one field of one) is `set_in_place`: the indices checked, then if `strata_list_owned(x)` (and the record unique, for a field) `strata_list_put` replaces the element and drops the old one; for `grid[r][c]`, each inner row in place when only its list holds it, else a copy of the row put in its slot; otherwise a copy (`strata_list_set`, new in core.list and runtime.c). A declared refined element type checks only `v` at run time. Measured: a million `xs[i % n] += 1` on n = 1,000 and n = 1,000,000 both well under a second (case `build-element_assignment_is_in_place`, also through a field and a grid); a copy each time would be 10^12 element copies. Not done: `d[k]` / `d[k] = v` on dicts (reading `d[k]` isn't accepted yet: `at` has no `Dict` version; `d[k] = v` would be `insert`, which the desugaring can't pick without types, so `set_at` would need a `Dict` overload in each pass), swapping two elements (`xs[i], xs[j] = xs[j], xs[i]` needs tuple assignment to existing variables), the refined-element shortcut for a field's list (`s.xs[i] = v` with `xs: List[Positive]` checks the whole list at run time when unproven), in place through deeper fields (`a.b.xs[i]`: a copy).
  **The plan to replace `llvm.rs`** (laid out with the user 2026-11-22; they group the items into PRs; compile-time code, the FFI and the JIT may be taken by other agents, as they don't depend on the code generator yet). Each item compiles what `llvm.rs` compiles, tested by the same behaviour as `strata build`'s executable and, for a program that ends normally, nothing left alive under `STRATA_MEMORY=check`.
  - **A. What nearly every program uses.** (1) Lists (step 4). (2) Type arguments from the checker: `check.typed` writes the type arguments of each generic call into the tree (needed by 3). (3) Generic functions: one compiled copy per set of type arguments. (4) Closures and function values: lambdas that capture (counted closure blocks), calling a function-typed value, a named function as a value. (5) The compiled prelude: the list built-ins written in Strata (`map`, `filter`, `fold`, `sort_with`, …), compiled with every program (needs 1, 3, 4); with comprehensions. (6) Core's other built-ins (`core/builtins.strata`): strings, math, parsing, each a runtime function or prelude code, partial ones checking their `requires`. (7) Sets and dicts: sorted lists with a structural comparison helper per type, which also gives `<` on records and sums. (8) Format specs in f-strings.
  - **Group B, in progress** (branch `codegen-b`, one PR as A was, #309). Built, each tested as before (`tests/codegen.rs`): (B9) methods and traits: a method resolves by the receiver's type, the type's own `impl` first, else the one trait implemented for it with the method (the `impl`'s or the trait's default), compiled as a function instance named `Head.method` on the call stack (`Self` the type, the `impl`'s type parameters first); static methods (`Type.f()`, `T.f()` in an instance); bounds through instances; a type's own `Display` / `Debug` wherever values are shown; trait values as a block `{ table, value }`, the table (drop, show, debug, eq, the trait's methods, supertraits' first) of wrappers calling the instances. (B11) `r!`, `o?` (the `Err` or `None` returned as it is), `except` (the error bound), `??`, a `main` returning a `Result`. (B12) `mut` parameters and `mut self` (a slot the caller fills with the place's value and writes back), field assignment (the path's blocks rebuilt, the rest shared); there is no index assignment in the language. (B13) unions as `llvm.rs` lays them out (a record's or variant's block is itself, any other member boxed `{ box tag, value }`), coerced where a union is wanted, `match` / `is` / `==` / show / order / drop by member, the matched variable narrowed in its arm and by `if x is T`, methods by the union's own `impl` else each member's; `distinct` types as one-field blocks (`D(x)`, compared as their value: one allocation more than `llvm.rs`, the same behaviour); aliases. (B14) contracts at run time with `llvm.rs`'s messages: parameters', results', declared variables' (and their assignments'), fields' and constructed values' types (core's refinements from `core/types.strata`, which `main` now passes along, the program's, inline `where`s, list elements), `requires`, `ensures`, loop invariants; refined records and sums compiled (the checker's model leaves them out); what `verify` proved is left out as `strata build` leaves it out (user, 2026-11-23): `strata build --strata` verifies what changed, as `build` does, and hands `compiler codegen --proofs FILE` the facts (`llvm::proofs_text`, a line each: `proven F CHECK`, `open F CHECK`, and `entry F argument P` / `entry F requires I` for an entry check every call proves, by `llvm.rs`'s rule); codegen names its checks as `verify` does and leaves out those proven in the function being compiled (results, `let`s, assignments, constructions, `assert`s, invariants, indexes, a division's check for 0), never in helpers or lambdas. Fixed on the way, in group A's code: lists copied by the runtime held only some element kinds (unions, trait values and closures were missed). (B10) iterators: core's code (`core/iter.strata`) compiled with the program (its names global, as core's types are; only what is used is compiled); `for` over a type with an `Iterator` impl is `loop { match mut it.next() { Some(pat) => body, None => break } }` on a copy (invariants at the head); a trait's default method gets the trait's parameters as the `impl` gives them (`FiniteIterator[B]` for `Mapped`); a built-in called on one of the program's types with a method of that name calls the method (`map(it, f)` from a comprehension, `it[i]`, `it[a..b]`), `set`/`dict`/`join` of an iterator are core's `set_of`/`dict_of`/`join_of`; a call's type arguments are also bound through its bounds (`I: FiniteIterator[T]`). A parameter of a trait with type arguments (`it: Iterator[Int]`) given a value of a known type is compiled for that type; a `let` of such a type keeps its value's own type; left in B10: such a value in a field or a collection (`{ it: Iterator[Int] }`: its element type is lost). (B15) capabilities and std: whole programs (a script, or a package without dependencies: each module named by its path; functions by their qualified names), the std modules they use read from `std/` (`main`'s `std_loading` / `std_roots`, again after `derive`s are expanded until nothing new is named; the terminal when the console is used), core's `core/text.strata` too; a capability is a record with no fields (it holds nothing; its methods an `impl`); `main` given its console (the terminal, as a trait value), capabilities, `RunMode` (`Running`) and options (`from_args`, `--help` exit 0, bad arguments exit 2); std's `host_*` operations (results made `Option`s and `Result`s as `llvm.rs` makes them); a parameter of a trait's type given a value of a known type is compiled for that type (`llvm.rs`'s `impls`: `mut self` changes kept); `restore` keeps the functions registered in helpers; closures capture through `??`, `?`, `except`, `mut`. Fixed on the way: `strata_host_run` leaked the OS's message when a program can't start (runtime.c); `deps.module_files` took a package's `.strata` cache folder for a module. Left in B15: options need compile-time code (std.cli's `valid` takes `comptime t: Type`: C19); packages with dependencies; `host_code_snapshot`; a `mut` trait value's `mut self` changes through its table (a value of a known type is compiled for its type instead). (B16) concurrency, as `llvm.rs` compiles it: `scope` (the runtime's; its tasks end before it does), `spawn` and `spawn supervised` (a closure run as a task, the senders it captures shared; run, drop and a supervised task's panic functions per result type), `await` of a handle, a tuple or a list of them, channels (`channel()` / `channel(n)` with core's `core/channel.strata` methods; one runtime object both ends hold; of `()` when nothing is ever sent), `select` (recv, send, await, and `clock.after(d)` timers: the system clock's, a virtual clock's, any other clock asked again while the select waits), `race` (supervised arms too); in a program that starts tasks, a cancel point after each wait and each call into compiled code (an unwound callee: the caller leaves too, through the function's `unwound` exit), a fault becomes `strata_panic` (a supervised task's panic leaves; elsewhere it ends the program), runtime functions that may panic in a supervising program are cancel points (those returning numbers). The Strata checker on the way: `select` and `race` arms inferred (operations, names, bodies), `spawn supervised`, a tuple `let`'s type recorded (`channel()`'s ends), `Sender`/`Receiver` known (core's methods on them), `for` over an iterator gives what its `next` gives, and `fill` writes types inside tasks and arms. Codegen: a variable declared with a type not known whole (`mut xs := []`) takes the type an assignment gives it (its drops then drop its contents). Options without refined fields compile (`derive CommandLine`). A supervised task panicking inside a runtime function returning a pointer (strings) leaves at once too (its result held after the cancel point). (B17) the C FFI, as `llvm.rs` compiles it: an `extern c fn`'s body converts its arguments (sized integers, `Float`, `Bool`, `String` copied to a NUL-terminated string and freed after the call, handles, `Option` of a handle as a pointer that may be NULL), calls the C symbol (declared once, widened as clang declares it), converts the result back (NULL for a handle is a fault); a `mut` parameter is an out-pointer read back after the call; contracts as for any function; an `extern c type` is a handle (`extern:Name`), never counted, shown `<Name>`, equal when the same pointer; `build --strata` links a package's `[native]` C. (B18, part) tests: `compiler codegen --tests` compiles the program's own tests (each a function of no parameters given what it asks for as `strata test` gives it: the console, `Testing`, a capability; no frame of its own), each as `strata.test.N` after a line naming it; `strata test --strata` runs them with `strata test`'s JIT harness and prints its report, `--compare` compares it with `strata test`'s. Mocks: `mock T` as `llvm.rs` makes it (a trait value of a table whose methods check their `requires` and generate a result until their `ensures` hold, shown `<mock T>`), with `llvm.rs`'s value generators ported (a generator per written type and hints: integers and floats within the intervals a refinement's predicate gives, `ints::hull` ported, strings and lists of the lengths it gives, tuples, lists, sets, dicts, options, records and sums with a variant picked at random, the smallest past depth 3, refined types and aliases retried until they hold, a trait value a mock); not yet: a string refined by a regex has no samples (it is retried like any refined value). Fuzz drivers: `compiler codegen --fuzz F,G` emits `strata.fuzz.N` as `llvm.rs`'s (arguments generated, handed to `strata_fuzz_keep`, the call; functions and loops count steps); `verify --jit --strata` uses them for a script (the shapes from `llvm::shapes_of`, a method's wrapper compiled by Rust), with the same verdicts and counterexamples. Fixed on the way: postconditions see the parameters. (C19, part) compile-time code: `derive`'s expansion (in Strata) already evaluates it while compiling; what it leaves compiles: a `match` on a constructor written out keeps only the arms that can match it, and a field of a type not known (a variant never made) reads as `()`; so `derive CommandLine` options compile, refined ones included; an unfilled hole (`?name`) compiles as `llvm.rs`'s fault; `matches(s, pattern)` runs std.regex's engine (`match_code`, a bad pattern a fault saying why), where `llvm.rs` makes a literal pattern's matcher while compiling (the same answers, sooner: not yet). With these, `build --compare` agrees on every example package with a `main` (hello, fuzzy, wc, page). (C22, measure) `build --compare` also agrees on the build tests' programs (data, lists, numbers, sized), after: a refined type's name as a pattern (`Positive =>`) tests its refinement, built-ins such as `to_string` as values, and a generic call's lambdas compiled after its other arguments (which bind the type parameters; making a closure does nothing). tests/codegen.rs checks all of these with `build --compare`. (C20, step 1) `compiler jit FILE|DIR`: the program compiled by compiler/codegen and run in the compiler's own process by LLVM's LLJIT through its C API (`compiler/c/jit.c`, `extern c fn jit_run_main`; the runtime's symbols are the process's own). (its exit code the process's); tested against Rust's executables (tests/codegen.rs, jit_runs_programs_as_built_ones_run); `strata run --strata --jit [-- ARGS]` runs a program through it; compile-time code needs no JIT in the Strata pipeline (`derive` runs it with compiler/eval while compiling: running it on the JIT would only be faster); next: compile-time code and `--trace` on it. Left in B: (18) `--trace` (its table of value shapes, kept in Rust); within the items built, (a refined record is re-checked after a field assignment now; elements' refinements in sets and dicts, and an `Option`'s or `Result`'s payload, are checked now, with `llvm.rs`'s messages).
  - **B. The rest of the language.** (9) Methods and traits: inherent and trait methods called statically, generic bounds, a type's own `Debug` / `Display` in `print`, trait values (vtables); the checker says which implementation each call uses. (10) Iterators (`core/prelude.strata`'s `Iterator`, lazy adapters, `for` over any iterator; needs 9 and 4). (11) Failures as values: `?`, `!`, `except`, `??`, a `main` returning a `Result`, `Panic`. (12) Changing values in place: `mut` parameters, field and index assignment (copy on write through `strata_unique`). (13) Unions (boxed members, member patterns), `distinct` types, sized integers. (14) Contracts at run time: refinements, `requires`, `ensures`, invariants, with `llvm.rs`'s messages; what `verify` proved left out (its proofs handed over). (15) Capabilities and std: `main`'s capabilities, std's modules compiled with the program (`host_*` from the runtime): whole-program compilation (modules, qualified names, std and core read from their packages). (16) Concurrency: `scope`, `spawn`, `await`, `select`, `race`, `supervised`, channels. (17) The C FFI (`extern c`). (18) Tests and tools: `test` definitions and `strata test`'s harness, mocks, `--trace`, `verify`'s fuzz drivers.
  - **C. Compile time and switching over.** (19) Compile-time code (`comptime`, `comptime for`, derive), which runs while compiling. (20) LLVM from Strata: the JIT through `extern c` and `llvm-c`, for `run`, `test` and compile-time code (needs 17). (21) Precompiled std (dynamic under the JIT, static in executables). (22) Coverage and switch-over: `build --compare` over `tests/programs.rs`'s programs and the examples as the measure; then `--strata` as `build`'s default, then `run` and `test` (after 20), then `llvm.rs` deleted.
  - **Group C: done, and what is left** (reviewed 2026-11-23 against main through #332). Done (#324): C19's compile-time code for the Strata pipeline (`derive` evaluates it with compiler/eval while compiling; holes, `matches`, refined options compile); C20's JIT from Strata (`compiler jit` over llvm-c, `strata run --strata --jit [--trace]`); C21 (std's library precompiled once: `build` links it, `compiler jit` loads it); C22's defaults (`build` and `strata --file F test` through compiler/codegen, Rust as the fallback, `--rust` / `STRATA_BACKEND=rust`). What is left, so that `src/llvm.rs` (the Rust code generator, 9.6k lines) can be deleted; `src/llvm/jit.rs` (runs IR through inkwell) and `native.rs` (reads and writes values by shape) are not code generators and may stay until the JIT and value reading move to the compiler too. Each item is a place that still calls `llvm.rs`:
    1. **`strata run`'s Rust paths** (src/cmd/run.rs): done (branch `codegen-calls`) but for `run --rust` itself. `run` (a program's `main`, `--trace` too) is compiler/codegen's and the compiler's JIT's by default (`compiler jit --name NAME --errors FILE`, with the proven-check list, std's precompiled library and a package's `[native]` C loaded); it no longer runs `main` on compiler/eval (`run --strata` still does). `run f ARGS` goes through the calls mode: Rust checks the probe as before (`invalid input` for a wrong argument), then writes it again with its result's `debug` text as its own, stored, in a module file of its own; `codegen --call FILE` adds it to the program and compiles `strata.call.0`, which calls it and hands the text over (`strata_ct_keep`); `jit_run_call` (compiler/c/jit.c) runs it as a test runs (`strata_test`) and prints what it printed, then the result, or the fault (its stack without the probe) or `error: invalid input: …` for an argument whose refinement the function refused on entry, with `run`'s exit codes. What codegen doesn't cover falls back to `llvm.rs` with a `note:`, and `--rust` / `STRATA_BACKEND=rust` ask for it (cargo's tests, pinned to Rust, still check `llvm.rs` there; tests/codegen.rs `run_goes_through_the_compiler_jit` compares the two). Found on the way: a program using std's precompiled library crashed in the compiler's JIT (`examples/wc --help`), because the JIT looked symbols up in the whole process first and the compiler, a Strata program built with std itself, holds functions of the same names (`strata:std.cli.where_of`) compiled by `llvm.rs`; the libraries `jit_load` loads are now searched before the process. And the compiler's cache key now covers its C (`compiler/c/*.c`), so a change to jit.c rebuilds it. Left for 3 and 4: the calls mode takes one call; several calls and values handed in rather than written (`derive`'s compile-time code, the viewer's calls) come next.
    2. **`strata test`'s Rust paths**: without `--file` (a codebase), `--with-deps`, `--jit`, `--interpret`, and the fallback (`llvm::compile_tests`, `compile_tests_except`). The default path already uses codegen's IR with `jit::run_tests`. Next: `codegen --tests` for a codebase and with dependencies' tests, and the precompiled std there too.
    3. **Compile-time code in Rust's `derive`** (src/derive.rs: `llvm::compile_comptime` + `jit::comptime`): what the Rust front end still evaluates while expanding (`comptime e`, derives' code). Either a codegen mode compiling calls whose results come back as Strata literals (the `debug` helpers, as verify's step 2 plans), or the Rust front end handing expansion to compiler/derive.
    4. **The viewer's calls** (src/serve.rs: `llvm::compile_calls`): the same calls mode as 3.
    5. **`build-core`**: `core/rt.strata`, the runtime written in Strata (#321's allocator), compiled by `llvm::compile_core_rt` into the second bootstrap stage. compiler/codegen would need raw memory and `builtin static` (core.mem). Coordinate with whoever owns core.rt. **compiler/codegen compiles core.rt (built, branch `core-rt-codegen`; asked by the user 2026-11-23, after finding the default `strata build` linked the C allocator)**: `Ptr` lowers as an uncounted handle (`extern:Ptr`, as llvm.rs's `handle`); a `builtin static` is a zeroed global `@"static:NAME"` made when first named (`statics`, `static_block`); core.mem's 17 builtins are llvm.rs's IR (`mem_builtin`: `raw_alloc` / `raw_reserve` / `raw_size` / `raw_free` / `raw_fail` / `raw_fill`, `ptr_offset`, `address`, `ptr_at`, plain and atomic loads and stores). `compiler codegen --core-rt` compiles `core/rt.strata` as a library with `generate`'s new `bare` (no call-stack frames, no cancellation points, as Rust compiles it: the allocator runs in every allocation) and adds each `strata_...` function under its C name (`with_c_names`, `zeroext` for a `Bool` result), as `llvm::compile_core_rt` does. The default `strata build` (compiler/codegen) now links it with `-DSTRATA_CORE_RT`, as `build --rust` links Rust's compile of it; a default build's executable has core.rt's `strata_alloc`. Test: tests/codegen.rs `core_rt_compiled_by_codegen` builds programs with core.rt and with the C allocator and compares them: output; check mode's summary (`N allocated, M freed, K live`: a free core.rt didn't count shows only there, which a mutation check confirmed the test catches); and, after a fault, the live objects; through small objects, blocks past the small sizes and tasks. **#357's verify fixes on the Strata side (branch `verify-fallback`)**: `compiled_drivers` no longer compiles each fuzz driver alone when they can't all be compiled together; `compiling` splits the set in halves until a name that can't be compiled is alone, as Rust's `jit_fuzzers` does since #357 (test: tests/smt.rs `verify_fuzzes_around_what_cant_be_compiled`). #357's wrapper fix isn't needed here: compiler/codegen compiles `List.make(n)`, a static call on a generic impl's head, binding `T` from the type its result is wanted at (checked: it runs, `Some([7])`). Measured on std.toml (17 s against Rust's 3 s, the same report but for generic impls' static methods, which compiler/smt doesn't encode yet: "not covered"): the JIT is a small part; most of the time is waiting for solver processes (`poll`), SHA-256 in Strata (`binary.sha256` over big integers) and allocation. Compiling fuzz modules without optimizing (as Rust's fuzzers do) changed nothing measurable, so it isn't done. Left: core.rt is compiled in the root module (its definitions' module set to `""`): under its own module, `core.rt`, library mode left every function out (reaching something not compiled under qualified names; not investigated). The rest of runtime.c (strings, integers, lists, faults, tasks, I/O) is still C: porting it to core.rt is the next project.
    6. **`build`'s fallback and `--emit-llvm`**: the programs compiler/codegen still refuses. Known gaps: a trait value with type arguments in a record field (`{ it: Iterator[Int] }`); `mut self` changes made through a trait value (a value of a known type is compiled for its type instead); `verify`'s proofs for a package (only a script's are handed over); the compile-time regex matcher (only faster). Measure: `build --compare` over tests/build.rs's programs, as for the examples.
       **The compiler package compiles with compiler/codegen** (built, branch `codegen-parity`, 2026-11-24): `compiler codegen compiler` reports nothing it can't compile, and the compiler it makes (linked with runtime.c, core.rt and c/jit.c) writes the same IR for the compiler package as the Rust-built one, byte for byte (`strata build compiler` still uses `llvm.rs`: building it with codegen needs codegen built first). What it took: `Self.f(x)` in a method's body; the checker's types for `mut xs := []` given by a `mut` argument, for a tuple pattern met before its type is known (`Some((lo, hi))`), and for `f(a) + f(b)` of a closure whose result is decided later; a dictionary's `remove` stored as `std.fs.remove` (a module importing `Files`): the first argument's method, else the built-in; a capability trait (`Files`) a trait in codegen's model, and a constructor's fields made as their type wants (`Some(mem_files(…))` for an `Option[Files]`); a `match` statement whose arms give `()` and a value; dyn tables' parameters no longer clashing with fresh names. The fuzz generators: generic functions at the types Rust's `test_instance` picks (unbounded `Int`, bounded the first implementation of its bounds, else a mock), unions (one member picked), functions (one returning a value generated once, whatever it is given), `s.graphemes()` from std.unicode; an `impl` of its own module's trait (`Toml` in std.toml) found by the trait's qualified name, and a package's modules named by their headers in `codegen` (std's `std.toml`, so std isn't loaded twice), and `T.check(x)` (a type's refinement check, `llvm.rs`'s `$check`: only a refinement the type's name carries, since a refined alias given as a type argument is its base type in codegen), so std.toml's 63 wrappers on built-in types compile. A `distinct` member of a union is checked on its field (its regex was run on the block, which took gigabytes). A concrete value is made a trait's value where a trait type is wanted (a record's field, an annotated variable, a list's elements and `push`, an `if`'s or `match`'s branches), a `mut self` method through a trait value writes back into it, and a trait value matched against a constructor (`Log { lines }`) or `is` tests its table (examples/tasks' `run print.demo` crashed on these). Found, not fixed (Rust only): `llvm.rs` crashes on a `distinct` member of a union (`name_of` in tests/codegen.rs), and says `false` for `o is Tally` on a trait value holding a `Tally`.
       **Open (for the user)**: Rust's verify doesn't fuzz a function taking a function (`skipped: not compiled`); compiler/codegen now generates function values, so `verify --compare` differs on those (`apply` in tests/smt.rs). Keep fuzzing them, or skip as Rust does?
    7. **verify's compiled code** (`llvm::compile_fuzz`, `jit::fuzzers`, the refinement probes, replay): taken by *`verify` in Strata*, step 2 (above), through compiler/codegen and the compiler's JIT.
    8. **Small shared pieces** to move out of `llvm.rs` before deleting it: `Proofs`, `proofs_text`, `ALL_CHECKS` (verify and the store use them), `RUNTIME` (build), `native::int_expr` (smt, derive).
    Then: tests/build.rs and the `.cargo/config.toml` pin (`STRATA_BACKEND=rust`) go over to compiler/codegen, and `llvm.rs` is deleted.
  - Across all of them, `compiler/check` hands over what only inference knows: type arguments (2), the implementation each method call uses (9), lambda parameter types (4), what narrowing proved (13). Optimisations (constant blocks, stack blocks, `strata_concat_owned`, reuse in place) are another agent's, later.
- **Self-hosting, step 1: the checker in Strata runs from `strata`** (asked by the user 2026-11-22; built, branch `check-compare`): `strata --file F check --compare` runs the Rust checker as before, then the compiled `compiler/` program's `check` on the same script (with `--std` the repository's `std/`), and prints the errors only one finds (`only in rust: …`, `only in strata: …`, matched line for line in any order), exit 0 when they agree, 1 when not, 2 when the compiler package can't be found or built. The compiler stays a program, not built into `strata` (the user's decision): `strata` finds the package (`STRATA_COMPILER`, else a `compiler/` folder holding `strata.toml` in the current folder or above, else above the `strata` executable, i.e. the repository it was built in), builds it once with `strata build --no-verify` into `~/.cache/strata/compiler/<hash>/compiler` (`STRATA_CACHE`), the hash covering the package's and `std/`'s `.strata` files and settings and the `strata` executable (path, size, time), so any change rebuilds it (the first run takes about a minute). Scripts only for now: `compiler check` reads one module and the std modules it imports, not a package's other modules, so `--compare` on a folder or a package says so and exits 2. On the repository's scripts (`examples/*.strata`, `tests/data/*.strata`, 10 files) both checkers agree (all check). Next: (1) packages, module by module, once `compiler check` reads a package's sibling modules (`std_imports` generalised to the package's own imports); (2) `--compare` over the repository's tests' error programs (`tests/check.rs` keeps only lines holding chosen keys: the full outputs differ where the Strata checker doesn't cover a rule yet), to list what is left; (3) `strata check --strata`: only the Strata checker's errors, as a step to making it the default once `--compare` agrees everywhere; (4) then the same for the other front-end commands (`lex`, `parse`, `resolve`) and `compiler/codegen` behind `build`.
- **Self-hosting, step 2: `--strata` and `--compare` on every command the Strata compiler implements** (asked by the user 2026-11-22; built, branch `check-packages`): `check`, `lex`, `parse` (`--stored`), `render`, `run` and `build` take `--strata` (the compiled `compiler/` program's matching command alone: `check`, `lex`, `parse`/`stored`, `text`, `eval`, `codegen` + clang) and `--compare` (both; the differing lines, exit 0/1, 2 when the compiler can't be found or built or doesn't cover the case), through one helper in src/cmd/compare.rs (docs/tools.md). `compiler check` now takes a package folder: all its module files checked together, with its dependencies and the std modules any of them imports as context, errors named per module (`core.run`) as `strata check` names them. On the repository's packages (`examples/{page,tasks,fuzzy,wc,hello,counter,todos}`) the checkers agree (no errors). Not covered yet: packages for `lex`/`parse`/`render`/`run`/`build` (scripts only), chosen definitions for `render`, chosen program arguments for `run` (`compiler eval` passes `one`, `two words`), programs `compiler/codegen` doesn't compile yet (e.g. `Result` values; `build` says so and exits 2); `verify` has no Strata implementation yet, so no flags. Next: `--compare` over the tests' error programs to list what the Strata checker misses, then `--strata` as the default once they agree everywhere.
- **`compiler/smt`, the verifier in Strata, step 1** (asked by the user 2026-11-23, after desugaring moved to Strata; built, branch `smt-core`): a port of `src/smt.rs`, the SMT encoding, started while the Rust verifier is still growing, since what it adds is mostly new cases, not changes to its core. Tested **by the solver's answers, not by the queries' text** (as codegen is tested by what programs do): `strata verify --obligations` (new) prints each function's obligations with the solver's answer (`proven`, `refuted`, `unknown`; those true whatever the values, which make no query, are `proven`), nothing run, no fuzzing or replay; `compiler smt FILE` prints the same from Strata, and tests/smt.rs compares them obligation for obligation (a test program covering step 1 whole, and the repository's scripts). A function using anything step 1 doesn't model says `not covered: …` (`Vc.gaps`) and is left out, rather than being modelled differently from Rust: so Rust can reword its queries freely, and only a changed answer shows. **Step 1** (`compiler/smt`): the core (terms and their simplifications as `and` / `or` / `ite` make them, unknowns with the reason they were made, facts under the path condition, obligations and `given`, the cone of influence, the queries as Rust writes them, string-length bounds included), values `Int` / `Bool` / `String` and their refinements (inline, the program's aliases and core's: `Natural`, `Even`...), operators (`div` / `mod` with "no division by zero", bit operations exact where SMT-LIB can say them, else bounded; comparisons, string `++`), `if` (branches merged with `ite`, variables merged too), blocks, `let` / `mut` (annotations checked, declared types kept after each assignment), `assert`, `return` (each exit checked), calls through the callee's contract (arguments' refinements and `requires` checked, result type and `ensures` assumed; `mut` arguments written back; the same call on the same values gives the same result), f-strings (their parts), floats unknown as in Rust. The solver runs as a process (`std.process`'s `Commands`; the script written to a file under the cache, all of a function's obligations in one run between `push` / `pop`, those it didn't finish each alone), as Rust's `run_batch` does. A model is a counterexample only when every parameter can be shown (none opaque), as Rust's `to_literal`. On the repository's scripts, every function covered agrees (7 of 73: the rest use records, sums, lists, loops or built-ins). **Step 2** (asked by the user 2026-11-23; built, branch `smt-data`): records, sums (`Option` and `Result` included) and tuples as SMT datatypes, as `data.rs` makes them: a parameter's or result's outer level made now, its structured parts terms of their datatype sorts unfolded one level when read (`unfold`: the selectors, a variant's constructor stated, the validity predicate of the term defined by its level's check, a recursive type checked to two levels), `ite` of two terms with their validity merged; constructors (with the type's refinement as an obligation, `constructing `X``), record updates, fields (of a record, a tuple, a field several variants share), `match` with constructor, literal, tuple and or-patterns, `let` patterns, `is`, `!` / `?` (the error leaves as an early return), `except` / `??`, distinct types, equality of structured values; the sorts declared (the datatypes as one group), strings read from datatypes named as Rust names them. A model is a counterexample when every parameter can be shown from it: the model's values parsed as s-expressions (`let`s expanded) and checked as Rust's `to_literal` / `sx_literal` would build them. On the repository's scripts, 16 of 73 functions covered, all agreeing (step 1: 7); tests/data/smt/step2.st, 32 functions. With #310 (verify-gaps) merged: a `match` with an arm the checker can't decide (a `where`, a refined type by name) has the obligation `some arm of the `match` matches` (the arms' conditions, or-ed), and a value of a type parameter is a term of an opaque sort (so two can be equal). Not yet: patterns naming a type (`Positive =>`, `is` with a type), which need unions' membership tests. **Step 3** (asked by the user 2026-11-23; built, branch `smt-lists`): lists, as the Rust encoding has them: a list is its length and how its elements are made (`Gen`: a checked type, a literal, `range`, a part of another list, `filter`, `map`, two branches' lists, a shift, a reversal, `+`, a datatype's list sort), so an element is made on demand, by position where the list has them (`element_at`); a list of a checked type has an element template at a placeholder position, shared by its copies (`template`, `@@i` / `@@oK`, unknowns that are functions of positions), and what is known of every element (`all` known true, a declared element type) reaches the elements made before and after (`know_all`); `all` / `any` are proven on an arbitrary element (`every`, a witness outside a scope, a quantifier inside) and give a quantified fact over positions (`forall_positions`); list equality by position in goals only (`eq_lists`); list sorts in datatypes (`T:List(..)`, `.len` / `.at`), dicts and sets by their size. Lambdas and named functions passed to `map`, `filter`, `all`, `any`, `find` are kept (`Func`), and run on an element where the call is made (their checks hold there). The builtins (`builtin`): lists, strings (`byte_len`, `byte_slice`, `index_of`, `parse_int`, `replace`, `chars`, ...), numbers, `option`, the faults of partial builtins, `len(s.code_points())`, and the rest as a fresh value of the result type its signatures share (core's `builtin fn`s, `builtins_of`). On the repository's scripts, 55 of 73 functions covered, all agreeing (step 2: 16); tests/data/smt/step3.st, 29 functions. One text difference left: Rust's verifier reads the checker's tree, where a lambda's parameter has its inferred type (`(w: String) -> …`), which `compiler/check` doesn't write yet (`typed` does it for `let`s), so tests/smt.rs compares obligations with lambda parameters' types left out. **Step 4** (branch `smt-loops`): loops as Rust runs them: `while` (its written invariants checked on entry and after each pass, the variables it changes made any values of their declared types, invariants inferred Houdini-style from candidates: the condition's comparisons, the returned variable's `ensures`, the `requires`, each changed integer against its entry value, 0, the integer parameters and the others), `for` over a list (an element of it per pass), `loop`, `break` and `continue` (the state after a loop merged from its exits). Inference asks the solver while encoding; the encoder can't run a process, so it asks for what it lacks (`ask`: the query becomes pending, the answer stands in as unknown), and `encode` runs the solver on what is pending and encodes the function again, with every answer so far, until nothing is pending: the last run sees the answers Rust's inline queries get. `for` over an iterator (which Rust's checker lowers to a `loop` calling `next`) is not covered yet. On the repository's scripts, 61 of 73 functions covered, all agreeing; tests/data/smt/step4.st, 12 functions. **Step 5** (branch `smt-methods`): method calls as Rust makes them: a method declared by exactly one trait and by no type of its own goes through that trait's contract (its `requires` checked, its `ensures` assumed); any other method gives a value of its result (it depends on the receiver), `mut` arguments written back; a module's function called as a method (`x.f()` for `m.f(x)`) when it is the only function of that name; built-ins first (Rust's prelude methods have qualified names). A function's name as a value runs its contract on unknown arguments; generic functions through their contracts (a type parameter's values are opaque terms). On the repository's scripts, 65 of 73 functions covered, all agreeing; tests/data/smt/step5.st. **Step 6** (branch `smt-transparent`): calls seen through in contracts (their body unfolded), for the functions Rust's termination analysis makes transparent: not recursive, finishing (no `while` / `loop`, or marked `terminates`; callees that finish) and unable to fail (no `requires`, no `mut` parameters, no division but by a non-zero literal, no `assert`, no refined constructor, no early exit, callees that are transparent or total builtins) (`transparency`, `Seen`). Where Rust's answer depends on a termination proof not ported (a loop's bound, a recursion's measure) the call is not covered (`NotKnown`) rather than guessed; methods in contracts too (Rust sees an implementation through only for a call its checker resolved to a trait, `Trait::m`, which the stored tree doesn't record). examples/math is now covered whole. **Step 7** (branch `smt-regex`): `matches` with a literal pattern, through std.regex's `smt_model` (the SMT-LIB regular expression, exact or bounded from both sides for a large Unicode class) and `relations` (inclusion, disjointness of two patterns tested on one string), as Rust's `regex_model` / `regex_relations` call them; a call through a trait method is named as Rust's checker names it (`Db::load`; a core trait's method by its name alone). On the repository's scripts, 70 of 73 functions covered, all agreeing. **Step 8** (branch `smt-std`): std. `compiler smt` names a module's functions as Rust does (`std.utf8.encode`, in messages too); a qualified type's variants are qualified the same way (`std.json.Integer`: constructors, patterns, parents); assignment to fields (`x.f.g = e`: a record's field replaced, a sum's value made unknown, the declared type and the record's refinement checked after); function values (`FnOf`: a value of a function type, called, gives a valid value of its result type; a lambda as a value runs its body on unknown arguments). On std, 382 of 385 functions covered, all agreeing (tests/smt.rs checks std.utf8, std.time, std.http, std.process; the whole of std takes some minutes). **Step 9** (branch `smt-iter`): `for` over an iterator, as Rust's checker lowers it (`mut $iter := xs; loop { match mut $iter.next() { Some(p) => body, None => break } }`), on the value already made; a method's `mut` parameters count as changed for a loop (`method_params`). std: 383 of 385 covered, all agreeing. **Step 10** (branch `smt-mcontract`): method calls written as Rust's checker resolves them (`elaborated`: `Trait::m` when one trait outside core declares `m`), so their text matches (`a.Sized::size()`); in a contract, such a call is seen through the receiver's implementation when that is transparent (no loop to prove, cannot fail), else through the trait's contract. std: 384 of 385 covered, all agreeing; the scripts: 71 of 73. **Step 11** (branch `smt-stdlib`): all of std as context for a script, as Rust's resolver sees it (a method of a module the script doesn't name: `s.graphemes()`); the scripts: 72 of 73 covered, all agreeing. **Step 12** (branch `smt-static`): a method called on a type (`T.empty()`) is a static method, the type only picking the implementation; the scripts: 73 of 73 covered, all agreeing (`frequencies` up to its lambda's parameter type, which compiler/check doesn't write yet). **Step 13** (branch `smt-union`): union values (`VUnion`: a tag and one slot per shape, keyed by `val_key`): values of different shapes merged (`if big { Square {…} } else { Circle {…} }`), union types as datatypes (`union_groups`: members grouped by shape, a sum's variants one slot whose constructor is one of them), membership (`shape_test`, `in_type`) for patterns naming a type (`Positive =>`) and `is` with a type, a union's fields, equality, constructor tests, its members used as `Int` / `Bool` / `String` / a list, and a `match` narrowing its variable to the one member an arm can match. tests/data/smt/step13.st (tests/cli.rs's union program and a few more): 26 of 26 functions agree. std: 385 of 385 covered, all agreeing; the scripts: 73 of 73. Calls whose transparency needs a termination proof: done by `compiler/termination` (verify in Strata, step 1); tests/data/smt/step14.st checks them (a `while` bound, a measure of a recursive function passed as a value, `loop`, an `Iterator`, `terminates`): 29 of 29 agree. **Step 15** (branch `smt-lambda-types`): each lambda parameter written without a type gets the one the checker infers, when fully known (`check.lambdas_typed`, Rust's `annotate_lambdas`), for the verifier's tree: each untyped parameter is marked by its node's path before inference (as `instantiated` tags calls), the contracts inferred with the body (each `requires`, the body bound to `it`, each `ensures`), and the type written where it holds no type variable or parameter. The obligations' text is now Rust's (`(w: String) -> …`): tests/smt.rs no longer strips lambda types. The scripts: 73 of 73 agree, text included. `verify --strata` / `--compare` come once it covers whole programs.
- **Compile-time code on the compiler's JIT, its value read from memory in Strata** (asked by the user 2026-11-23; built, branch `derive-jit`): the self-hosted pipeline ran `comptime e` on compiler/eval, an interpreter, which is slow on real computation (`comptime parse(...)` of a 60-item JSON document: 10.4 s on eval, 0.17 s now; `comptime fib(27)`: 2.25 s, 0.38 s; derives, many tiny evaluations, were already fast and stay on eval). Now compiler/derive's `expand_with` takes a runner, and compiler/main's `comptime_value` compiles `e` as a function of its own (its type from `check.typed`), adds it to the program as a call (`strata.call.0`, codegen's call entry, the same as `run f ARGS`'s), loads it with `jit_fuzz_load` and runs it with `jit_call_case` (as a fuzz case runs: `strata_fuzz`, a fault caught, within 5,000,000 steps), then reads the value from memory by its type (codegen's `read_back`, from the same state `generate` builds: `new_gen`) and writes it in with that type (`{ let strata_value: T = …; strata_value }`: a literal alone may not say it, `[1, "x"]` of a `List[Int | String]`). What codegen can't compile yet falls back to eval (`not_compiled`). **Reading memory: `core.values`** (the user's idea: not all of core.mem for the compiler, an excerpt): `Raw` (an address) with `word`, `at`, `byte`, read-only, written over core.mem; only core and a package whose strata.toml says `read = true` under `[values]` (compiler/strata.toml) may name it, in both checkers (once per definition); `verify` never runs a function taking one (`Assumed`), and the JIT-running `comptime_value` takes the disk as `jit` does so verify doesn't run it. Fixed on the way: `strata_test` (runtime.c) reset the call stack's depth and never restored it, so a host compiled with frames (the compiler, running a call or a fuzz case in its own process) then left frames below zero and wrote before the frame arrays: it now keeps the host's frames aside; a dict's and a set's value written in after desugaring as the calls `desugar` makes (`dict([...])`, `set([...])`: codegen never sees `Dict { … }`), for eval's values too; `check.typed` writes union types (`written_type`). Found: `llvm.rs` loses the elements of a `List[I64]` in the code it compiles (the reader collects `Raw` records instead; llvm.rs goes away). tests/codegen.rs `comptime_runs_on_the_compilers_jit` (every kind of value, as eval computes it, and nothing left allocated). Left: a derive's own compile-time code on the JIT, batching several values per module (each now re-types and compiles the program: fine for `comptime e`, too slow for a derive's many small pieces), std.unicode's tables (inside an iterator method codegen can't reach yet), `strata verify` in Strata treating `Raw` the same way.
- **`verify` in Strata: the command, termination, and running compiled code** (asked by the user 2026-11-23, after `compiler/smt` covered every function of the scripts and std). What `strata verify` does beyond the SMT encoding was still Rust's: the command (the cache of verdicts, `--require`, the proven-check list `build` uses, `--cross-check`, laws and counterexamples: `src/verify.rs`, `src/cmd/check.rs`), the termination analysis (`src/termination.rs`), and everything that runs compiled code (fuzzing, the refinement checks of literals, replaying the solver's counterexamples). Three steps, each a PR, each compared with Rust as the steps of `compiler/smt` were. **Step 1, termination (built, branch `verify-strata`)**: `compiler/termination`, a port of `termination.rs`: the call graph (functions, implementations' methods by `verify`'s labels, a trait method's node reaching every implementation of it, a call of the implemented method on a field of `self` left out), each `while` loop's candidate bounds proven to go down (obligations `compiler/smt` now makes as Rust's does, kept apart from `--obligations`' list: `Vc.loops`, `term_obs`), structural recursion (a smaller piece of a parameter at every recursive call), measures (single candidates, then pairs compared in order: `rec_sites`, `measure_check`), mutual recursion, `terminates`, the verdicts combined with the callees', and transparency (finishes and cannot fail). `compiler/smt`'s contracts now see through exactly what it finds transparent (`seen_through`), so no call is left `NotKnown`. Compared with `strata verify --termination` (new: each function's and implementation method's verdict, and `transparent` / `transparent (assumed)`), by tests/smt.rs's `termination_as_in_rust`: all 18 scripts (tests/data/smt/termination.st covers loops, measures, Ackermann's pair, mutual recursion, `terminates`, trait methods) and all of std (504 functions) agree. What matching Rust needed: a `for` over an iterator the encoder knows to be no list makes a function non-transparent (Rust lowers it to calls of `next`), and one over a type that isn't a `FiniteIterator` may not end; the methods of core's code modules (`core.iter`, `.channel`, `.text`, `.task`: `map`, `get`, `len`) are the builtins' to Rust's analysis, core.types' (`to_string`) are methods; `setting("key")` is a literal (Rust's `derive` writes it in); `matches` and the channel builtins aren't total (their contracts aren't written yet), nor is raw memory; an implementation's method is judged with its trait's contract. One Rust bug fixed on the way: the checker's `$self_type(T, call)` wrapper (a receiver's type, passed along) made a function non-transparent. Not ported: Rust's cache of std's loop bounds and measures between runs (`StdCache`); the Strata side analyses what a file reaches in 1-3 s. A type's own methods (`impl T { … }`) are not nodes (Rust knows them by qualified names no call uses). **Step 2, running compiled code (built, branch `verify-run`)**: fuzzing and the probes run in the compiler's own process. `compiler/codegen`'s drivers with `--fuzz-text` hand each generated argument back as code (`shown`, then `strata_fuzz_arg`, a new runtime.c function; Rust's drivers keep `strata_fuzz_keep` and its shapes), so nothing needs `llvm::shapes_of`. `compiler/c/jit.c` compiles the drivers once (`jit_fuzz_load`) and runs one case of driver N (`jit_fuzz_case`: runtime.c's `strata_fuzz`, seeded, within a step budget, looked up with `dlsym` since the C file is also linked where there is no runtime), then reads the fault's kind, message and stack and the case's arguments. `compiler fuzz FILE [F,G]` ports `fuzz_capped` (cases passing, its own precondition's discards, `could not generate`, holes as incomplete, counterexamples with their stack folded and cut as Rust's `short_stack`); Rust's new `verify --fuzz` prints the fuzzer's verdict alone, and tests/codegen.rs's `compiler_fuzzes_as_verify_fuzzes` compares them function by function (what codegen can't compile yet is left out: compiled together first, each alone when that fails, as Rust says why per function). On the scripts, every function codegen compiles gets Rust's verdict, after three fixes to codegen's generators and drivers: a sum's variant picked in declaration order (it was name order), a generic type's written arguments kept (`Option[Pos]` generates a `Pos`), and the step past the budget taken in the frame just entered (the `Limit` stack's count). Left: Rust generates strings for a `matches` refinement from the regex (`type Code = String where matches(it, "[A-Z]+")`), codegen's generator only retries; fuzzing generic functions (Rust picks instance types), functions over types with shared fields, lambdas whose types compiler/check doesn't write, `graphemes`. The literal-refinement checks (`refinements_in`: `make_port(70000)`, `Line { qty: 1200 }`, `Even(3)`) are now in `compiler check` too: a probe per literal (`let _: T = <literal>`) compiled with the program and run in the same JIT, with Rust's messages; codegen now checks `let _: T = e` (it skipped the check for `_`). The replay of the solver's counterexamples and the solver-made inputs need `compiler/smt` to give a model's values as literals: step 3. **Step 3a, the command for functions (built, branch `verify-cmd`)**: `compiler verify FILE [--cases N] [--seed S] [--cross-check N] [--require R] [--checks]` ports `check_fn` / `solve_then_fuzz` (no race) and the report: compiler/smt's answers with Rust's messages (`obligation_answers`: `the solver found a possible counterexample: x = -1 (depends on …)`, `… it could not express`, `the solver gave up`; `why` from the cone's unknowns), a model's values as code (`literal_of` / `sx_literal`, Rust's `to_literal`), and Rust's examples mode (each list parameter's first elements made in `function`, their facts set aside, the query asked again with them when a model can't be shown: `open_input`, `input_elems`, `body_with`), so the solver's counterexamples include lists (`[0], 0` for a division by zero). A refuted obligation is replayed (a probe binding each value at its parameter's type, then the call, compiled with the program and run in the JIT, which now keeps several modules: `jit_fuzz_load` gives a handle); what is proven is cross-checked on 20 cases within 200,000 steps (only code shown to terminate); the rest is fuzzed (`fuzz_run`, with a cross-check's budget setting aside cases that run out of steps). Inferred invariants and what is seen through assuming `terminates` count among the checks (`Vc.invariants`, `relied_on`). The report: `✓ ~ ✗ ○ -`, `not proven:`, `may not terminate:`, `terminates if …`, `--require proven|terminates|no-recursion` as `policy:` lines. tests/smt.rs's `verify_as_in_rust` compares it with `strata verify --no-race --no-cache` on one script covering the features (tests/data/smt/verify.st): identical. Known difference: Rust fuzzes on threads with smaller stacks, so very deep recursion overflows there before the step budget runs out. Left for 3b and 3c: trait methods and laws (wrappers), the solver-made inputs (`inputs`, when random values rarely meet a refinement), the verdict cache and the proof list `build` uses, packages, `strata verify --strata`, the race. **Steps 3b and 3c (built, branch `verify-rest`)**: `compiler verify` checks what Rust's `verify_source` does, in its order: a type's own methods (as functions `Type.m`), each implementation's methods (its own or the trait's default, with the trait's contract first, `Self` its type, its type parameters at `impl_instance`'s types, under `impl_label`'s labels; mocks' and capabilities' assumed; a method only the trait declares fuzzed), the trait's laws (`law_wrapper`: its result the law, `ensures`d; `law N does not hold for T with …`), and a `derive`'s copies of its trait module's functions (`std.cli.value.for.Options.26`: the derives are expanded, compiler/derive, before verifying). Methods and laws are run through wrappers (`strata_verify_K_m`, `verify_wrappers`) added to the program for both the solver (elaborated) and codegen's drivers (`codegen_with`'s extra definitions). What matching Rust needed: compiler/smt sees a static call on a concrete type (`Money.empty()`, a record's name is also its constructor's) through that type's implementation in a contract, as Rust's hidden type argument lets it; compiler/codegen checks a trait's contract in each implementation's method (llvm.rs's `method`). The verdict cache is the Strata side's own (decided: simpler than reading Rust's JSON; `~/.cache/strata/verify-strata/checks`, a file per check, the verdict as fields separated by 0x1e), keyed as Rust's by label, closure hash (from the module files' headers: each entry's code hash and dependencies, so nothing is encoded again), cases, cross-check, seed, the solver's *name* (not its version: `run` is shadowed in compiler/main by `eval.run`) and std's hashes; `(cached)` and `--no-cache` as in Rust. The proof list is written in `codegen --proofs`' form (`proven F CHECK`, `open F CHECK`) to `verify-strata/proofs/<path hash>.txt` or `--proofs FILE` (rewritten whole each run, so it is the code as it is; `build --strata` still uses Rust's list). A package (a folder) is verified as a script is, its modules named by their paths. `strata verify --strata` now runs `compiler verify` (the old `--strata`, Rust's verify with compiler/codegen's drivers, is gone: `FUZZ_SCRIPT`, `fuzz_ir`), and `--compare` compares it with `verify --no-race`. tests/smt.rs's `verify_as_in_rust` compares tests/data/smt/verify.st and verify_traits.st (traits, a default, static methods, a law that holds and one that doesn't, a type's own methods, `derive Json`) and the example package `hello` (`derive CommandLine`, its std.cli copies): identical; `counter` and `todos` agree too with `verify --compare`. `compiler verify --load LIB --precompiled NAMES` loads std's precompiled library into the JIT (as `jit` does; `verify --strata` passes it): without it the drivers' module compiles all the std code it reaches, and LLVM's store merging took ~170 s on std.web's (`counter`: 190 s, 2 s with it). Found on the way, then fixed (branch `jit-fix`): under the JIT with std's dynamic library, `examples/hello` faulted in `std.cli.where_of` (`no match arm for Some("1 <= it && it <= 10")`; `run --strata --jit` too; a static `build` worked). The cause: the JIT resolved what the program declares from the process first, and the compiler's executable has its own `strata:std.cli.where_of` (the compiler is a Strata program using std.cli, compiled by Rust's code generator, whose values are laid out differently). So the program's call reached that copy, not the library's. compiler/c/jit.c now looks in the libraries `jit_load` loaded first, then the process (the runtime). Also: the cached compiler's key (`compare::built`) didn't cover the package's C, so a change to `compiler/c/jit.c` wasn't rebuilt. It now covers `.c` and `.h` files. Test: tests/codegen.rs `jit_uses_the_std_library_not_the_compilers_copies`. A call of an `extern c` function is now assumed as Rust's verifier does: compiler/smt calls it by its contract (`verified_with` puts it among the functions, its body a hole, so neither it nor what calls it is seen through). **Sized integers in compiler/smt (built, branch `sized-ints`)**: Rust's model, ported. A value of `U8` … `I64` is an `Int` tagged with its width (`word`, a `VDistinct`), known to lie in its range (`word_range`, a fact wherever one is made: `shape`, `from_term`). Its arithmetic, bit operations and negation are wrapped modulo 2^bits (`wrap_term`, two's complement for a signed one), and comparisons are of the integers. `to_int` gives the integer, `to_u8` obliges it to fit ("`to_u8` of an Int that fits in `U8`"), and `wrap_u8` wraps. tests/data/smt/verify_sized.st gives Rust's report (`verify_as_in_rust`). Also: a C function with no `requires` is total to compiler/termination, as to Rust's analysis, so what calls it may be seen through; and `compiler termination` lists a file's own definitions, not a derive's copies. Left: `examples/fuzzy` still differs, for two reasons. (1) The JIT doesn't load a package's `[native]` C. (2) `closest` is proven on the Strata side but only fuzzed by Rust: seeing through `text.distance`, Rust leaves the C function's result unmodelled ("`to_int` is not modelled"). Rust is the weaker side there. Fixed (branch `plain-calls`): compiler/termination's `cannot_fail` took a plain call `print(x)` for a trait method whenever some trait has a method of that name (std.io's `Console.print`), so `fuzzy`'s `main` wasn't transparent. A plain call is now a function's or a builtin's, and a trait method only when written `Trait::m`, as in Rust's analysis (which says `print(s)` is transparent with std.io imported too).; **Lazy std and a package's C (built, branch `lazy-native`)**: compiler/smt, compiler/termination and `compiler verify` read std as `codegen_with` does, only the definitions the program reaches (`std_reached`: `load` from `std_roots`, again after a `derive`'s expansion until nothing new), not every module (asked by the user: Rust loads std lazily too). `compiler verify` on a package builds its `[native]` C (strata.toml's `sources`, `libs`, `include_dirs`, `lib_dirs`) with clang (`STRATA_CC`) into a shared library (`deps.native_library`, in the cache) and loads it before compiling the drivers, as Rust's JIT does, so fuzzing and replay call the real C functions. `examples/fuzzy` now differs from Rust only where the Strata side proves more (`closest`). the entry facts of the proof list (`entry F argument P`); the solver-made inputs (`Vc::inputs`, `or_solver_inputs`); the race; selecting definitions (`verify NAME`).
- **`strata verify` is the Strata verifier** (asked by the user 2026-11-23; built, branch `verify-default`): `strata verify` runs `compiler verify` unless `--rust`, `STRATA_BACKEND=rust` (cargo's tests) or `--json`, or when no compiler package is found; so does what `build` and `run` leave out. What it needed: the program checked first (`file_errors`, shared with `compiler check`; the literal probes compiled against std's precompiled library, which took `check` on examples/todos from 3 minutes to seconds; `compiler check --load/--precompiled`), selecting definitions (`--only`, Rust's `view::select` as `select_names`: names, `prefix*`, `module:`, `slice:` over `store.graph`, `main.NAME`, a module's name), the package's `[verify]` rules (`deps.verify_policy`, Rust's `Policy::covers`), exit codes (1 on a counterexample or a rule broken, 2 on a mistake in the arguments), the package around the current folder without `--file`, and std itself: a package's modules are named by their headers (std's `std.json`), `load_package` reads std as std (its modules `std.…`, not loaded again as a library) and compiler/check's `method_clashes` compares module-qualified names, as Rust does (it took `term.paint` for a clash with `Console.paint`): `compiler check std` went from 4177 errors to none. The proof record keeps each check with its definition's closure hash (a definition not verified in a run keeps its record while its code is unchanged; `--quiet` for `build`, which verifies first and says `verified N changed: …`); `compiler proofs FILE` gives what is current, with Rust's entry facts (`entry F argument P`, `entry F requires I`: `refs_of` ports `llvm::Refs`), for `codegen --proofs` (`build`, `run`; parsed back into `llvm::Proofs` for the `llvm.rs` fallback). Each definition's status (its worst verdict, Rust's `Verdict::rank`/`tag`) is kept by closure hash in `verify-strata/status` for a viewer, listed by `compiler verdicts FILE` (Rust's `view` still reads Rust's). `--obligations`, `--termination`, `--fuzz` and `--smt` go to `compiler smt`, `termination`, `fuzz` (all with `--only`) and `smt --queries`. A check compiler/smt can't encode yet is now reported as skipped with the reason (`- f  skipped: not covered by compiler/smt yet: …`, not kept between runs), so `--require proven` counts it; this showed `std.json`'s `List[T]`, `Option[T]` and `Dict[String, V]` `from_json_at` not covered (`T.check(x)`, the refinement check on every type): compiler/smt now gives it a `Result[(), String]` nothing is known of, as Rust's verifier does. Compared with Rust: the scripts' reports, `hello`, `todos`, `counter`, a `[verify]` package, `std.utf8` and `std.json` with `--require proven`, the four modes (tests/smt.rs `verify_chooses_checks_and_rules_as_in_rust`, `proofs_and_statuses_follow_the_code`). Left: the race; `--json`; `--smt` shows the obligations' queries, not termination's; a package's proofs are recorded but `build` and `run` still pass none for a package (as before); compiler/termination takes `to_string(x)` for a builtin where Rust takes it for `Display`'s method, so `--termination` calls `label` (tests/data/smt/verify.st) transparent (no verdict changes); `strata.toml`'s `[verify]` naming no module of the package is still only `strata check`'s error; all of std through `compiler verify` not yet timed to the end: a module with a generic implementation (`impl Json for List[T]`) makes `compiled_drivers` compile each driver alone (as Rust's `jit_fuzzers`), about a whole-std compile per target (another agent is fixing that path).
- **`compiler verify` as fast as Rust's** (asked 2026-11-24; built, branch `verify-speed`): (1) compile-time values kept between runs as Rust keeps them (`$STRATA_CACHE/comptime/KEY.strata`, a module file of one function holding the value), keyed by this executable (c/jit.c's `jit_self_stamp`: path, size, time), core's and the prelude's code, the module and every definition the code reaches (`reached`: generous where a name is unclear; a fast 128-bit hash, `fast_key`, since SHA-256 in Strata took 60-80 ms on the 150 KB closures of std.unicode's tables); a definition's `comptime` values are asked of the runner together (derive's `fold_comptime`), so their closure is hashed once. (2) core, the prelude and core's code read once per run (`Shared`), and verify's program read, loaded with std and expanded once (`Base`): each compile (literal probes, drivers, replays) adds its definitions (`with_extra`), unless they hold a type, a derive, `comptime`, a refinement in a type's arguments, or need more std. (3) solver queries in parallel: `smt.run_scripts` runs many scripts as separate processes, one per core (`sh` + `xargs -P`), the same script once; `encode_all` asks the questions of many encodings round by round together (termination's `while` loops; verify's targets, 32 at a time, `solved_together`), `answers_all` makes their batches, singles and second asks together. Verdicts identical. (4) `text_key` and smt's `text_hash` on wrapping `U64` (the verdict, status, proofs and native-library file names change once). std.toml: 10.2 s → 5.1 s with cached verdicts, 22 s → 8 s with `--no-cache`. Then (branch `smt-scan`): compiler/smt's `body` looked for each string term (`str_terms`, thousands in a large function) in the whole obligation script with the prelude's byte-by-byte `str_find`; the script's words and word pairs are now read once (`keys_in`) and a term is searched for only when its inner words and pairs are there (`occurrence_keys`: the same terms as before), and `str_len_terms` checks a byte before slicing. `strata --file compiler verify --no-cache 'termination.*'`: 191 s → 112 s, same verdicts; what is left is mostly the solver's rounds in termination's invariant search. Each run still reads std's files three times (the base, `file_errors`' package, `verified_with`): decoding (SHA-256 checks included) was about 0.3 s of a 7.5 s cached std.toml run in a sample, so not worth sharing yet.
- **Self-hosting, step 3: the gaps `--compare` finds** (asked by the user 2026-11-22; built, branch `compare-gaps`). `check --compare` over every error program of tests/check.rs (31) and over the programs of the tests in tests/cli.rs, tests/build.rs and tests/resolve.rs that run `strata check` (38 programs). Before: tests/check.rs 118 lines only in Rust (111 of them Rust's own cascade: a program redefining a prelude type, `type Option = …`, made the prelude's code fail against it; 1 a message going on over indented lines, which `--compare` cut; 1 a test parameter of a generic type; 1 the arm after an unknown constructor) and 1 only in Strata; the other tests 68 only in Rust and 10 only in Strata (a test's name and an `extern c` function's not qualified in a package, `std.io.Console` not taken as `Console`, `List[Circle]` not read as `List[Shape]`). Fixed: Rust keeps the prelude's definition when a program redefines it (the error stays, the cascade goes); `--compare` keeps a message's indented lines; the Strata checker now reports the resolver's errors (`use` of a missing module, of what a module doesn't have or keeps private, a foreign function or a mock used outside its place, a name of a module not used; what a std module lacks is left out, since std comes as headers), checks a folder without strata.toml as a package, names tests (`test "core: name"`) and `extern c` functions as `strata check` does, reads type arguments member-wise (`List[Circle]` is a `List[Shape]`), takes a constructor no type has as matching anything (as Rust does, on a sum's value) and asks a test's parameters to be `Console`, `RunMode` or a capability, as Rust does. After: tests/check.rs agrees fully (0 and 0: `same_errors` now compares every error, in any order, no key filter); the other tests 52 lines only in Rust, 0 only in Strata: matches over refined or literal values (9: what isn't covered, `where 3 <= it && it <= 5`, an arm no `Positive` matches), other typing (11: operand and body types in generic or overloaded code, field defaults, `has no field` on a sum), `derive` (8: what can't be JSON, CommandLine), traits (6: `for` over what isn't a List/Set/Dict/Iterator, calls needing a bound, `mock` of a non-trait), `mut` (5: receivers, `mut` arguments needing the exact type), compile-time refinement violations (4), `extern c` types (3), concurrency with clocks and senders (3), capabilities made by the program, `RunMode` hidden, compile-time code using variables (3). The repository sweep stays clean. `--compare` runs the compiler from the repository's root (it reads `core/` from there), the files it is given made absolute. `lex`, `parse` (`--stored`), `render` and `run` take `--strata`/`--compare` on a package now: `lex` and `parse` on each module's text (as `render` writes it), `render` on each module file, `run` on the program packed into a bundle (`strata pack`). On the repository's example packages `lex` and `parse` agree on every module; `run` agrees on `page`, `counter`, `todos`, `tasks` (the Strata side now words `no main function` as Rust does); `hello`, `fuzzy` and `wc` ask for options (`CommandLine`), which `compiler eval` can't give yet; `render` differs on every module that names another module's definitions, since `compiler text` prints a stored tree's names as stored (`std.web.Html`, `util.twice`) where `strata render` writes them as short as the module's imports allow. When each command's Strata side could be the default: `lex` and `parse` now (they agree on every script and package module tried); `check` once the 52 gaps above close (refinement-aware matches and compile-time refinements need the solver from Strata, the largest piece; the rest is checker work); `render` once `compiler text` shortens names through the module's imports (the inverse of resolving); `run` once `compiler eval` gives `main` its `CommandLine` options and the chosen arguments; `build` once `compiler/codegen` covers `Result` values and packages.
- **Self-hosting, step 4: `lex` and `parse` default to the Strata front end; the cheap `check` gaps closed; `render` agrees on packages** (asked by the user 2026-11-23; built, branch `frontend-default`). `strata lex` and `strata parse` (`--stored` too) now run `compiler/`'s lexer and parser (the compiled `compiler` program, as `--strata` did); `--rust` runs the Rust ones, which tests/lex.rs, parse.rs and resolve.rs compare against, and which `--compare` runs for its Rust side. Without a compiler package (`strata` installed away from the repository) they fall back to Rust.
