Tested (`tests/toml.rs`) against the `toml` crate on curated documents and, with `STRATA_TOML_CORPUS`, the 711 UTF-8 files of toml-test's 1.1 list: same values, same refusals (all 493 invalid ones), `inf`/`nan` aside.
**`Json`'s methods renamed, the `to_json` builtin removed** (user, 2026-11-22): `encode` / `decode` are `to_json` / `from_json` (`to_json_at`, `from_json_at`, `from_json_with`), as `Toml`'s are `to_toml` / `from_toml`, so the names say the format and a type can implement both. The builtin `to_json(x)` (any value in an older tagged form: `{"Some":"x"}`) is gone from the compiler (`types.rs`, `llvm.rs`'s JSON writer and its trait-table entry, the runtime's `strata_json_int` / `strata_json_str`) and from `compiler/` (`builtins`, `check`, `eval`); `std.http.ok_json` takes a `T: Json` and writes `std.json`'s form (500 with the message when `to_json` fails), so `examples/page` derives `Json` for `Language`.

**`derive Toml`** (asked for by the user 2026-11-22, "like JSON"; built, branch `toml-derive`): `derivable(Record | Union) trait Toml`, `std.json`'s machinery adapted. Decisions: records carry no `type` key (a configuration file, not a message; a record in a union written out, `A | B`, gets one so it can be told apart); `Option` only as a field, `None` left out (TOML has no null); the methods are named for TOML (`to_toml`, `from_toml`, `from_toml_with`, `read_toml`, `write_toml`, derived `to_toml_at` / `from_toml_at`) because a type implementing two traits with the same method can't call it (`Json` and `Toml` together); `UnknownKeys` is `std.json`'s; `[toml] unknown_keys` in `strata.toml`; `Date` / `Time` / `DateTime` impls. Found on the way: derive was minutes slow on nested records (a `DateTime` field: 2+ min to store): `derive.rs` keyed every compile-time `Type` value by its printed, laid-out text, and remade a type's value on each access; keys are now `Debug` text and values are made once per type (4+ min → ~30 s for the test's program, cold).
Not yet: the tools reading `strata.toml` through it (they still use the `toml` crate, `src/package.rs`, until that code is Strata's); a writer that keeps comments and layout (editing `strata.toml` in place).

### Doc comments on union members (2026-11-23; built, branch `variant-docs`)
A member a union declares takes a `///` doc comment above its `|`, as a field does (`Variant.doc`, a format change: every file with a union re-encoded). Docs are left out of the hashes, printed by `render`, and given to derived traits (`std.typing`'s member `Type.doc`): needed for `std.cli` commands, whose help is their members' docs. A `///` after the last member is the next definition's. Not done: a type shown inside a union (`render`'s inlining) still keeps its own doc out of it (it stays separate when it has one).

### `print` and command-line options (decided with the user 2026-11-20; built, branch `worktree-stdlib-print-cli`, stacked on `worktree-stdlib-io`)
- **Capabilities stay** (user, asked explicitly): I/O is reached through `Console` / `Files` / `Environment` values; the value is the prefix (`files.read…`, `console.write…`). Plain ambient `fs.read(path)` functions were considered and rejected.
- **Except `print`**, which is a prelude builtin, available with no `use`: shorthand for "the console of wherever the code runs". To the terminal under `strata run` (both `main` and `run <fn>`) and in a built executable, into a buffer per test under `strata test` (printed under the failure only), nowhere under `verify` and fuzzing. A plain call `print(x)` always means the builtin, even where an imported trait has a `print` method (`resolve::PRELUDE_FNS`); `console.print(x)` is the method. A failing `assert` no longer prints a second time when it re-evaluates its sides for the message.
- **`CommandLine`** (`std.cli`) was first a marker trait with no methods (user's choice over "any record in main"), read by `src/cli.rs`; since `cli-derive` it is a derivable trait written in Strata (`derive CommandLine for Options`), with the same rules and messages. A `main` parameter whose type derives it is read from the arguments: `Bool` → flag, `Option[T]` → `--name value` / `--name=value`, `List[T]` → remaining positionals (one at most), anything else → required positional in field order. `T` = Int / Float / String / a refinement of one / a choice (variants without data, written in kebab case). Every refinement is enforced; errors name the option and exit 2; `--help` comes from `///` docs on the record and its fields. `strata check` rejects a `CommandLine` record with fields a command line can't fill.
- **Fields can have `///` doc comments** now (`Field.doc`; parsed, printed, ignored by hashes like other docs).
- Empty trait and impl bodies print as `{}`.
- **Done (branch `worktree-stdlib-files-names`, stacked on #16):** `Files` methods are `read`, `write`, `append`, `exists`, `list`, `make_dir`, `remove`. Two general rules made that possible (`Checker::by_receiver`, recorded as `Target::Other` / `Target::Builtin` and written by `elaborate`):
  - a method name is matched by its short name against the traits of the value it is called on, whatever module they come from: method names are qualified by module at resolution, and a scope maps `write` to only one of `std.io.write` / `std.fs.write` (the last import), so `console.write(s)` used to resolve to `Files.write`. Same rules as #13 otherwise (one candidate: it; several: the existing errors);
  - a trait method named like a builtin doesn't hide the builtin: on an Int/Float/Bool/String/List/Dict/Set whose type has no trait declaring the name, `d.remove(k)` / `remove(d, k)` is the builtin.
  - Also: `types::test_instance` never picks a capability (Terminal, Disk, Process) for a type parameter. The cross-check from #10 had fuzzed `fn f[C: Console]` with the real `Terminal` (the host gate stopped it, but it was reported as a prover bug).
- Still to do: module aliases `use std.fs as fs` (user: yes, but lower priority since the capability value already prefixes calls). Built since (branch `cli-std`): short names, commands as a union, repeated options and optional positionals (see "The `strata` command in Strata").

Done: std's real `Terminal` / `Disk` / `Process` impls are verdict `assumed` (`Verdict::Assumed`, `≈`; `--require proven` accepts them and lists them; the viewer shows them neutrally). Since then `extern c` code is `assumed` too. Not yet / next: dependencies as `assumed`; `main` can't ask for a clock, random numbers, network or a process spawner; verification cache keys don't include std's hash (a std change only comes with a new binary). Next modules the user listed: strings (beyond the builtins), regex, math/linear algebra (NumPy-like), web UI (HTML/CSS generation and serving, per the batteries-included plan below).

## Viewer (built)

Doc comments are shown as the description in the right panel (and stripped from the code, `strip_doc` in `ui.rs`); the inspector has no source block; code items are plain code with a margin marker; bodies open inline (`has_body` in the snapshot; impls and traits now have a signatures-only level 1 in `view.rs`); the header has *Code* / *Type map* tabs and the code toolbar has *expand all*. The selected definition starts open.

Still open: **type map as a side panel** instead of a full tab. The user suggested it; ask before doing it.

Relations (decided): a definition has **dependencies** (what it mentions) and **references** (what mentions it). Implementing a trait is a separate relation, never a reference or a dependency: an `impl T for X` block is listed under *Implements* on `X` and *Implementations* on `T`, and its own trait and type are shown as *Trait* / *For*. The snapshot carries `deps`, `refs`, `impls` and `impl_of`. The type map shows dependency edges by default; *implementations* and *refinement types* are opt-in. There are no effects and no functions in the type map. The user wondered about a traits-only view, showing how traits work together; not decided.

### Viewer as a workspace (decided with the user 2026-11-22; built, branch `viewer-workspace`)

`strata ui` becomes the place to work with agents, read the code and review PRs. It stays the Rust viewer (`src/ui.rs`, `src/ui/index.html`); the viewer rewritten in Strata isn't ready, and isn't used. Three modes in the header replace the *Code / Changes / Type map* tabs:

- **Agents** (the default). Left: the agent sessions and a *new session* button, then the activity. Centre: the selected session's conversation over **ACP** (the Agent Client Protocol), streamed in. Right half: the code, with the card renderer; clicking a definition an edit named opens its whole slice there. Agents run in this checkout, in another worktree, or in a new one (made at `.claude/worktrees/<branch>` from the default branch); the panel shows the target as that worktree has it. **Strata commands are shown for what they are**, with no MCP server: `strata …` run through the agent's shell tool is recognised from ACP's `tool_call` / `tool_call_update` (`rawInput.command`): `strata edit` shows the text piped in (a here-document, an `echo`, or the file it reads), highlighted, with the definitions it names as links; `check`, `verify`, `test`, `run`, `render` show their output in a styled block; other tools are a collapsible block (a file edit as a line diff).
- **Code**: the code view and the type map (a sub-tab) as before, with the activity on the right (beside the selected definition's details, a tab).
- **PRs**: the list (`gh pr list`, by state); a page per PR with what GitHub shows: title, description, state, the **stack** (the open PRs below it and those stacked on it, as links), commits, checks and CI runs, comments and reviews, and the diff from where it left its base to its head: the structural diff for `.strata` files (one per package they're in), a line diff for the rest. **Actions**: comment, approve, request changes, merge. A stack merges top-down: the PR into its base, then the PR below into its base, down to the default branch; a PR that is behind is updated first (`gh pr update-branch`); if `gh pr merge` refuses (a stack), the merge API (`merge-async`), then auto-merge (merges once CI passes), which stops the run: the PR below needs it merged.

**The activity** (like GitLens) replaces the *Changes* view: a timeline by branch (the worktrees' branches first, the viewer's own first of all, then local branches with commits the default branch doesn't have, then the default branch's latest commits), then by commit, then by the definitions each commit changed (added, removed, changed, and whether the interface did), plus each worktree's uncommitted changes. Hovering an entry shows a summary of what changed; clicking a commit, a branch (from where it left the default branch to its tip, with its uncommitted changes) or a definition shows the structural diffs on the code (in the Agents mode, in the code panel); with nothing selected the code is shown plainly. **Live dots** mark the definitions agents are working on: from `strata edit` input (the names it defines), from the `.strata` files their edit tools touch, and from the target's definitions changing on disk; a branch whose worktree has a working agent has one too.

**History** reads git only (not `strata.log`); the binary format is taken as settled, with no backwards compatibility: a commit whose `.strata` files don't decode is shown as "can't be read (older format)". **Data** comes from the local `git` and `gh`; the viewer never holds a token.

How it's built: the server reads POST bodies now (agents started and prompted, permissions answered, PR actions); it answers only its own page (the `Host` must be `127.0.0.1:PORT` or `localhost:PORT`, an `Origin` must match, and a POST must carry `X-Strata: 1`, which another site's page can't send without asking first), since it can now run agents and merge PRs. `src/ui/agents.rs`: one subprocess per session (`STRATA_UI_AGENT`, else `[ui] agent` in strata.toml, else `npx -y @agentclientprotocol/claude-agent-acp`; the handoff's `@zed-industries/claude-code-acp` is deprecated under that name), a thread reading its stdout into a list of events the page polls (`/api/agents/<id>?since=N`); the client offers no file system or terminal, so the agent uses its own tools; permission requests are answered from the page, or allowed by the session (`[ui] auto_allow`, or a checkbox per session). `src/ui/activity.rs`: the timeline (each commit's definitions computed once; the whole timeline again only when a branch tip, a worktree's HEAD or its files change) and `/api/diff?base=&head=` between revisions or `wt:<path>` (a worktree's files). `src/ui/prs.rs`: `gh` (`STRATA_UI_GH` replaces it, for tests). `diff::diff` no longer resolves both programs once per definition to look verdicts up (19 s → 0.4 s for a one-commit diff of `examples/tasks`), and not at all when there are no verdicts. Tested end to end in `tests/cli.rs` (`ui_workspace_activity_agents_and_prs`: a scratch repository, a fake agent, a fake `gh`).

After the user tried it (2026-11-22): the agents' code panel is the Code mode's view itself, beside the conversation: the same selection, the list of definitions (*Symbols*, can be hidden), ⌘P opens a definition there, and the panel's width can be dragged (kept). A change to a doc is shown as `///` lines in the code with the words marked, and a definition whose doc (or contract, or signature) changed but not its body shows only its signature. `strata.log` is left out of PR diffs. Then (second round): **Agents and Code are toggles, not tabs**: each on or off (one at least); both on, the conversation is on the left and the code on the right; one alone takes the whole width. PRs stay a mode of their own. The look is quieter: lower contrast (the syntax colours' hues kept, a little darker), fewer boxes, code wrapped to fit its pane (no sideways scrolling). A tool call is one thin line, as in Claude Code: a dot for its status, its name (`strata edit`, `Read`), its arguments (an edit's definitions, as links); only the latest three of the current turn are open (what an edit was given, what a command printed, cut at 12 lines), the rest are closed and open on a click. A PR's page: title, then a *Conversation / Diff* switch, the description, comments and reviews, commits; the checks and CI runs are a compact list in the right column with the merge and review actions; the diff shows the `.strata` changes structurally and the other files folded, by line. Stacked PRs are grouped in the list (the top first). A PR's page shows at once from what was read before, and is read again in the background. Then (third round): buttons are words (bold, an arrow or a sign before them), not outlined boxes; the message box is the whole bottom of the conversation, and Enter sends; sessions allow their tools by default (`[ui] auto_allow = false` to be asked); the conversation has one reading width (760 px) in every layout; the Agents sidebar lists sessions only (one working in another worktree names its branch), and the activity is in the Code mode. A side repository with only Strata code, `anonx3247/strata-playground` (private), has PRs to try the PR mode on (#2 stacked on #1).

Not yet:
- a mini-map beside the agents' code panel (optional, the user said);
- SSE instead of polling (sessions are polled every 0.7 s while one works, 2 s otherwise);
- ~~sessions are kept by the server only while it runs~~ (built): the Agents sidebar also lists the Claude Code sessions saved for the repository and its worktrees (`~/.claude/projects/<folder>/*.jsonl`, the folder named after the working directory), with their titles and branches; one opens as a read-only conversation (its lines turned into ACP events), and *Resume* starts the agent with `session/load` (the agent replays it, then goes on). Loading isn't a pure read: Claude Code appends a few bookkeeping lines (an "away summary") to the saved file. Agents run in a process group of their own, so ending a session ends everything it started;
- images, audio and resources in prompts (text only) and agent modes (`current_mode_update`) aren't shown. Slash commands are (built): typing `/` offers the agent's commands (`available_commands_update`), sent as the prompt's text. Strata commands are recognised however the agent calls the binary: `strata`, a path to it, or a shell variable set to one (`S=…/strata; $S check`), only in a command's position (`ls ~/dev/strata` isn't one);
- live dots from a tool's files mark every definition of the module the file holds (a `.strata` file is a module); finer would need the agent's edit tools to name definitions, as `strata edit` does;
- PR review comments on lines (only PR-level comments and reviews), and editing or deleting a comment;
- the PR diff for `.strata` files needs both sides readable by this binary: a PR from before the format settled, or built with tree changes this binary lacks, shows why instead.

## The site: Strata, presented (built, `site/`)
- **Slides, not a scrolling page** (user, 2026-11-21): one full view per step, ← → (or space) through each slide's steps and on to the next; the address names the slide and step (`#session/5`). It replaced the long page whose ← → only worked over a stepper on screen.
- **No embedded viewers in the presentation** (user, 2026-11-21): instead, code typed out as it is written, beside a graph of the whole codebase (definitions in columns by how deep their dependencies go, edges for uses) that shows what changed, what must be re-checked, holes, verdicts and counterexamples. The docs keep their tour of the real viewer (Tools → The viewer), since that page documents the viewer itself.
- **The thread** (user, 2026-11-21):
  1. The problem: how an agent works in a codebase of text files today (animated): mapping it with searches, editing lines in many files, finding out what broke, writing hundreds of tests, diffs nobody reads.
  2. Three ideas: types as contracts, proving over testing, the code is the AST.
  3. Verification first:
     - contracts spelled out (refinements, `requires` / `ensures`, what the solver checks);
     - loops and termination: measures for recursion, bounds for `while`, invariants (inferred, or written with `invariant`), what a loop keeps being mostly a variable's type (Collatz), a `Sorted` list type proven by position;
     - tests that miss a detail, against one line;
     - many tests against one `ensures`;
     - fuzzing as many tests at once;
     - verdicts;
     - mocks (the keyword, so the slides say "mock", never "fake");
     - side effects assumed so the rest is proven (capabilities, `extern c`);
     - building a piece at a time (only the signature matters, a body change stays there, holes).
  4. The code is the AST:
     - text → tokens → tree → graph → execution (animated);
     - Strata stores the graph;
     - structural diffs (a long diff scrolling fast, then one rename);
     - changes following the graph (a stricter precondition, then holes);
     - several agents at once: every change is checked, and merges pair definitions, so they don't conflict;
     - views of just what an agent needs;
     - editing through text (parsed into a tree, checked, rendered back).
  5. More of the language, one compact slide each, recorded:
     - algebraic data types (variants, `Option` / `Result`, `match` with `where`, a non-exhaustive match refused);
     - traits (as in Rust, composition over inheritance);
     - value semantics with reference counting, and `mut` visible at declarations and calls (a call without it refused);
     - the everyday syntax;
     - `comptime` (tables computed while compiling), with derive built on it (CLI, JSON both ways);
     - concurrency (user, 2026-11-22; three steps, user 2026-11-23): `scope` / `spawn` / `await`, channels and `select`, then `race` and `supervised` tasks; stored with `strata edit` and run.
  - Each section opens with its title, which then moves out of the way.
  - No history of the page, nothing about unmerged branches, and wording without the usual AI idioms (claudisms.ai).
- **Everything shown is recorded** by `site/build.mjs` from the real CLI, the new-feature slides included (small packages stored with `strata edit`, so a snippet that stops checking breaks the build). Broken since the viewer workspace (#181): the build asks `strata ui` for `/api/changes`, which is gone (the structural-diff scenes need it moved to `/api/diff`); a single scene can still be recorded on its own.

## A release with nothing to install (v0.1; built, branch `toolchain`)
The goal (user, 2026-11-24): `strata` runs on an Apple-silicon Mac with no developer tools (no clang, Xcode or its command-line tools, LLVM or z3). What the compiler executable needs is in it or beside it:
- **LLVM linked in statically** (compiler/strata.toml `[native]`: Homebrew's static libraries, those `llvm-config --libs aarch64 orcjit irreader passes lto …` names, plus Homebrew's `libzstd.a`; zlib, libc++ and libSystem are macOS's). The executable links only `/usr/lib` libraries (tools/release.sh checks).
- **Objects made in the process** (compiler/c/toolchain.c, `toolchain_object`): IR text parsed, optimized with `default<On>` and written by LLVM's C API, for `arm64-apple-macosx13.0.0` on the `apple-m1` CPU (clang's default for that target, so executables and the shipped objects run on any Apple-silicon Mac from macOS 13 on, whichever Mac built them).
- **lld linked in** (`toolchain_link`: `lld::macho::link`, declared in the shim so building needs no LLVM C++ headers, which want C++17 while the package's C is compiled with the C compiler's default). Homebrew's lld ships only dylibs linked against libLLVM.dylib (161 MB, itself linked with Homebrew's z3 and zstd), so tools/build-lld.sh compiles lld's Common and MachO sources (llvm-project's release tarball matching Homebrew's LLVM, checksum pinned) into `target/lld/liblld.a`, about 20 s; its `InitializeAllTargets` is narrowed to AArch64 (a generated `llvm/Config/Targets.def`) so the compiler links one target's libraries, not twenty.
- **libSystem without an SDK**: executables link against `compiler/c/libSystem.tbd`, a TAPI stub we write (tools/libsystem-tbd.sh: what the runtime, core.rt and std leave undefined, plus libm, libc, pthreads and dyld names programs may reach), with `-Z` (no default search paths); dyld finds the symbols in the real libSystem at run time. tools/release.sh fails when a shipped object needs a symbol the stub lacks.
- **z3 shipped**: Homebrew's z3 binary links only libc++ and libSystem. `verify` runs `STRATA_SOLVER`, else `<exe>/../lib/strata/z3`, else `z3` on the PATH (main's `solver_of`).
- **Layout** (tools/release.sh → `target/release-dist/strata-macos-arm64.tar.gz` and `.sha256`): `strata/bin/strata`; `strata/lib/strata/` is its home: `VERSION`, `core/`, `std/` (sources, and `std.o`, `libstd.dylib`, `names`: std precompiled), `src/llvm/prelude.strata`, `runtime-lto.o` (runtime.c with `STRATA_CORE_RT` and core.rt in one module, optimized together at -O3: a user's build never compiles the runtime), `libSystem.tbd`, `z3`, `cache/` (the shipped read-only cache: see below).
- Measured: `bin/strata` 93 MB (stripped), the archive 44 MB. Homebrew's bottles (LLVM's static libraries, z3) are built for macOS 26, so the release itself needs macOS 26; the executables it builds need 13. Compiling std's 7 MB of IR at -O2 takes 18 s in the process against 7 s for Homebrew's clang (the time is in SelectionDAG's store merging; `llc -O2` takes minutes on the same input): only when a release is made. Not yet: the compiler finds its home relative to itself (today the current folder, or `STRATA_HOME`), `strata build`/`run` call the toolchain (compiler/toolchain, from the CLI port).
- **The shipped cache** (built, branch `ship-cache`): what a first run computes from std, core and the compiler alone, made by the final `bin/strata` (keys start with the SHA-256 of its bytes: made after strip and codesign, the binary checked unchanged at the end). Measured (2026-11-24, an empty own cache: `run hello`, `build wc`, `--file wc run`, `check`/`verify`/`test` on math, bank, orders, traits, builtins, todos, tasks, counter): every entry written is the program's own (`verify-strata/checks|status|proofs`, a derived `std.cli` parser's verdicts) except std.unicode's five `comptime` tables (`Graphemes.next`: grapheme breaks, Extended_Pictographic, the Indic conjunct classes; 32 KB), the same keys from any program reaching them. tools/release.sh finds them so, not by name: two warm-up programs with no code of their own in common each run check, test, run, verify and build with an own cache; what both wrote (same key, same bytes, not the scratch `smt/`, `run/`, `build/`) is shipped, plus `CACHE_FROM` if given. They save 0.1-0.3 s on a first `check`/`test` reaching graphemes. Entries for the examples (`hello`, `wc`, `math`) are not shipped: no user runs them, and they would hide what tools/release-check.sh measures. It checks that a program of the user's reaching graphemes writes none of the shipped entries (and does without them), and prints first-run timings with and without the shipped cache.
- **What a first run still paid that no cache held** (measured 2026-11-24, built, branch `first-run`): (1) the compiler's identity, the SHA-256 of its 86 MB executable, about 0.2 s of every run (60% of a 0.36 s `check`): now read from an identity file beside it (`HASH SIZE MTIME`: `lib/strata/IDENTITY` in a release, written by tools/release.sh after strip and codesign; `target/strata.identity`, by tools/bootstrap.sh), trusted while the executable's size and modification time (seconds, what `tar` keeps) are those it records; else hashed once and recorded in the own cache (`identity/KEY`, KEY of its path, size, time and inode). Keys are unchanged (still the SHA-256 of the bytes), so the shipped cache matches. `check` of math.strata: 0.36 s → 0.11 s. (2) std's precompiled library now holds its `impl`s' methods (non-generic `impl`s of a std type or trait, trait defaults included: `library_methods` in compiler/codegen; 324 → 477 names), so a program or a fuzz module declares `std.unicode.Graphemes.Iterator.next` and the others instead of compiling them with their folded tables: a cold `verify` of a program reaching graphemes (own cache with std's library only) 6.8-7.9 s → 0.55-0.6 s. (3) Scratch: solver inputs go to `smt/PID` of the own cache, removed when the run ends (`remove_scratch`, in `main` and in `jit_file` before the program runs, as it may end the process); `STRATA_KEEP_SMT=1` keeps them, in `smt/` as before. `run`'s `run/PID` (proofs.txt, the call, the native library) is removed by `jit_file` once compiled (`--scratch`). Left: a run killed (SIGKILL) leaves its `smt/PID`; `verify examples/orders.strata` with a fresh own cache runs past 300 s in LLVM (the JIT's instruction selection), before this change as after.

## The tools' output, readable (decided with the user 2026-11-24; built, branch `tool-output`)
The port to Strata had lost the colors the Rust tools had (the tag `last-rust`, src/color.rs, which colored each output line on its way to the terminal), no command highlighted code in the terminal, nothing paged, and code came out through two commands (`render`, `view`). Decided: color at the source, one highlighter for the terminal and the site, git's pager, one `view`.
- **Code highlighted** (compiler/highlight, `code(paint, text, capabilities)`): site/common.js's rules (`hl`) and palette (site/style.css), so code looks the same in both: keywords, types (capitalized), function names (a name before `(`), strings and f-strings, numbers, `//` comments, `///` docs, holes; and capabilities, with the traits they implement (`Console`), in a color of their own (teal; the site has none yet), from the names given. It works on fragments (a counterexample's call, a hole's goal, an assertion). `piece_at` cuts the next piece and is proven (each piece ends past its start, within the text: so `code` terminates and its pieces tile the text). The law `plain(code(p, s, caps)) == s` isn't proven: the prover knows `byte_slice` exactly only on ASCII strings, and lists of strings by their length (tried with offsets, an ASCII `matches` precondition and lists of characters); it is a `test` on samples (Unicode, unclosed strings, lone `?`, `/`, `f`).
- **Named styles** (highlight: `ok`, `bad`, `warn`, `input`, `accent`, `dim`, `hole`, `heading`, `strong`), color.rs's palette, on a `Paint` (std.term): in the compiler, not std, as nothing else uses them. Applied: `verify` (the verdict's symbol and words, the name bold, a counterexample's input as code), `check` (`error` bold red, where bold, code in backquotes highlighted; `ok:` green), `test` (passed green, failed red, an assertion highlighted, the counts), `diff` (headings, `+`/`-` lines, changed words, hashes, holes: `diff_line`, color.rs's rules for a diff), `log` (palette), `holes` and `status` (holes, goals and contexts as code), `view` (highlighted). Off when not a terminal, as before, so the golden cases didn't change for it.
- **A pager, git's way**: on a terminal, what is printed to read (`view`, `diff`, `log`, `holes`, `status`, `interface`, `builtins`, the help) goes to `$STRATA_PAGER`, else `$PAGER`, else `less -FRX` (`LESS=FRX` unless set). `--no-pager` (anywhere before `--`) and `STRATA_PAGER=cat` or empty turn it off. `check`, `verify` and `test` stream (decided), `run` and `edit` never page. compiler/c/driver.c's `driver_pager` spawns the pager with a pipe as its input and points standard output at it, waiting for it at exit; runtime.c's `strata_host_paged` makes standard output count as a terminal from then on, so colors are decided as if it were one (`less -R` keeps them). Case `cli-tool_output_pager` runs it on a terminal (`script`) with a stand-in pager.
- **One `view`** (`render` removed, no alias: pre-release): `view MODULE` prints a module as stored, imports first (they printed where they were added), the text `edit` reads back; `view SELECTOR --level 0|1|2` as before; `view` alone is the package's map; `--split DIR`, `--members` are `view`'s. A selector naming a module prints it unless `--level` is given; `main` is the root module, or the only one (a script, `--file a/b.strata`). Selector parsing is one helper (`view_args`), for a later `view LOCATION [--context N]`. `setup-git`'s textconv is `strata --no-pager view main --file` (rerun `strata setup-git` in an existing clone).
- **Rough edges fixed**: the help's leftover line of `ui`; parse errors show tokens as written (``found `)` `` for `found Sym(")")`); a failed assertion shows short names and `(left: 2, right: 3)` (was `std.io.printed(console)` and `left of == =`); `--trace` hides `run FN`'s call (`strata_run_call`); `diff` numbers patches and steps from 1 alike (`--from 1 --to 2` is patch 2, step 2; `--step K` counts from 1); `log` shows a rename as `↪ renamed  old → new`.
- Found on the way, not fixed (not this branch's): a test calling `std.io.printed(console)` on its `Console` parameter crashed with signal 11 in `std.io.printed`.

## Known gaps

- `compiler verify` (now `strata verify`'s default) behind Rust (checked with `verify --compare` 2026-11-23): no solver-made inputs when random ones rarely meet a refinement or `requires`; generic functions not fuzzed (`T` not given a type); strings for a `matches` refinement not generated from the regex; `graphemes` not covered by compiler/smt; a package's proofs not passed to `build`. Left aside (user): the race, `--json`, speed, nested quantified facts, what the prover doesn't model.
- A list holding both an `Option[Int]` and an `Option[Float]` (`[a, b]`) makes LLVM refuse the IR (a `switch` with duplicate cases, seen 2026-11-22). `float_from_bits` gives infinities (only a NaN is `None`), though a `Float` is never infinite.
- A `Bool` option whose default is `true` can't be turned off (no `--no-x`), and `javascript:` URLs in `std.web`'s `href` aren't rejected (both listed where they were decided).
- FFI is C only (`extern c`, see "Foreign functions: `extern LANG`"): `main` can't yet ask for a native module's capability, and there is no std SQLite module yet.
- `Files.read` (`host_read_file`) and `Console.read_line` don't check that the bytes are UTF-8: a file that isn't gives a `String` that faults later (`code_points` on it). Found with toml-test's `invalid/encoding` files; it should be an `FsError`.
- `d[k]` on dicts isn't accepted (`at` has no `Dict` version).
- `--json` is missing on `view`, `render`, `interface`, `status`, `done`, `test` and `run`.
- **Iterators into lists (STRATA-ISSUES #1/#9, decided with the user 2026-11-25; built in part, branch `issues-language`).** The checker converts a finite iterator to a list where a `List` parameter (functions, methods, `T.m`), a list beside `==`/`!=`, or a shown value (`print`, `to_string`, `debug`, f-strings) expects one: `check.listed_arg` records the argument, `check.as_listed` writes `to_list(..)` into the elaborated tree (`typed`, `instantiated` for codegen, `lambdas_typed` for verify), so codegen, verify and eval see an ordinary call; an iterator that may not end is an error ("take a part of it first"). String methods of traits (`bytes`, `code_points`, std's `graphemes`) are now typed (`check.string_method`; std's impls are named `GraphemeText for std.unicode.String`). Closed in a second step (branch `issues-language-2`): a value of an iterator type the checker didn't know (a `map`/`filter` result bound to a variable, repro i2) is converted by codegen where it is made a `List` (`codegen.converted`, which `wanted` runs for parameters, results, `let` annotations and now `List` record fields: `construct_want`), and the checker accepts a finite iterator wherever a `List` is expected (`check.fits`); verify sees the checker's `to_list` (case `lang-iterators_become_lists`). Left: `strata check` of a `--file` program doesn't type `s.graphemes()` (std's impl isn't in its tables there; run/build do). Open: P2 messages (unused/typo `from std.io use Console`: the stored `use` shows no items and the module isn't loaded, so resolve says `there is no module std.io`, the derived `CommandLine` `?no_default` hole, `--input X` for a positional, a warning for an `invariant` kept as a runtime check, variant names per module).
- **Messages from STRATA-ISSUES' P2 table (branch `issues-messages`).** Built: a `use` of std loads what it imports whether it is used or not (`package_roots`, `std_imports`), a bare `use std.io` doesn't say "there is no module" when std has it, and a name the module lacks is said from its header with the close ones (`std_use_check`: "`std.io` has no `Consle` (did you mean `Console`?)"); a derived `CommandLine`'s required field makes no `?no_default` hole (std.cli's `field_value` takes the default only under `if comptime f.default is Some`, else a "missing <x>" usage error the parser already gives first); `--input X` for a positional `input` says "`input` is positional, given without `--input`: `prog <input>`"; `build` warns once per loop invariant verify left open (`kept_invariants`: it stays a runtime check, run at every pass) and `verify` notes the same on its "not proven" line. Decided (user, 2026-11-25): two unions written with the same bare atom (`type P1 = Aa | Bb`, `type P2 = Aa | Cc`) are one type `Aa` that both hold, the same as declaring `type Aa` on its own and both unions naming it (`resolve.union_members`: a bare name is the type of that name when one is in scope); that is fine by design, so it is neither an error nor a warning (the warning the edit gave is gone, branch `checker-duplicates`). With payloads (`Aa(Int)`, `Aa(String)`) it is an error, in a single `--file` too, and the message says the name must be unique "whatever type it is in". Built on the same branch: a name given twice where it must be unique is a checker error naming the type (or function) and the name (`declarations.twice`, found when a merge left codegen's `Gen` with two fields `at` and `check` said nothing): a record's fields, a variant's record fields, a sum's variants, a function's, lambda's and trait method's parameters (`_` aside) and type parameters, a trait's methods, any impl's methods (before, only an inherent impl's), a record literal's or update's fields and a record pattern's fields (case `check-duplicate_names`).

Decided against (not gaps):
- **Explicit type arguments at a call** (`f[Money]()`; decided by the user, 2026-11-21): type arguments are inferred from the arguments and the expected type only.
- **Error conversion** (decided by the user, 2026-11-21): `!` only passes an error on when it fits the function's error type (the same type, a variant into its sum, a member into a union). No conversion functions, implicit or declared: errors stay explicit as types.

## Known limitations

- **Proofs cover integers, strings, records, sums, unions and lists (by position).** A loop keeps only the declared types and the invariants inferred or written for it; positions are lost through `filter` and `sort`; floats, dicts and sets are not modelled; trait laws are fuzzed only.
- Length conditions on a list are only decided as "empty" or "not empty" (`where len(it) == 1` with `where len(it) >= 2` needs a `[]` or `_` arm).
- **Verification is slow**: `verify` on `examples/tasks` takes about 15 s of CPU with an empty cache (10 s wall), std 19 s; fuzzed verdicts are keyed by `closure_hash`, and a `verify` by name in `compiler/` checks the whole program first (~25 s). See "`verify`'s speed".
- **Little is cached**: verdicts in `.strata/`, compile-time values and std's termination findings in `~/.cache/strata`, std's compiled library; no check results, and modules are read one at a time.

## Roadmap

1. **SMT verification, continued**: nested quantified facts (see "The verifier's gaps, closed"), and `assumed` for dependencies. (Values as SMT datatypes, trait laws, match coverage are built.) (Loop invariants, inferred and written, list contents by position, and `assumed` for capabilities and `extern c` are built.)
2. **Traits with laws**: e.g. `trait Monoid { … law assoc(a, b, c): … }` (built: laws are written in traits, proven by the solver where it can, fuzzed otherwise, `verify::check_law`).
3. **Agent-friendly CLI everywhere**: `--json` on every command, trace queries, and scoped workspaces and leases so several agents can work on different slices at once. The CLI *is* the agent API; no server is needed.
4. **Concurrency and iterators**: built (tasks, channels, `select`, `race` and cancellation, a `Clock` and timer arms; iterators with `FiniteIterator`). Left: a deterministic scheduler for tests, deadlock detection, cancelling blocking I/O, supervised scopes; `mock record` / `mock replay` for integration tests; deterministic replay debugging.
5. **Viewer**: ~~show which agent is working on which slice, and add a diff view for each patch~~ (built as the workspace: see "Viewer as a workspace").
6. **Structural records with row polymorphism**: "anything with `.id: OrderId`". Records are nominal today: a `B` with the same fields as `A` is not an `A`.
7. ~~A compilation backend~~ (built: LLVM, see "Compilation before transpilation"). A wasm build is still wanted for the web.
